Let the Jews say how the soldiers lost the King Whom they were guarding: why did the stone not preserve the Rock of life? Either let them give up Him Who was buried, or let them worship Him Who is risen, saying with us: Glory to the multitude of Thy compassions; our Savior, glory to Thee!

*Sticheron:*

Rejoice, ye peoples, and be glad! An Angel sitting upon the stone of the tomb, he proclaimed the glad tidings unto us, saying: Christ the Savior of the world is risen from the dead [p40LA] and filled all things with fragrance: Rejoice, ye peoples, and be glad!

*Sticheron:*

An Angel indeed brought "Rejoice!" unto the Full of Grace before Thy conception, O Lord; and an Angel also rolled away the stone of Thy glorious tomb at Thy Resurrection: the former declaring emblems of joy instead of sorrow, and the latter proclaiming unto us the Master, the Giver of life, instead of death. Therefore we cry unto Thee, O Benefactor of all, Lord, glory to Thee!

*Sticheron:*

The women poured out myrrh with tears upon Thy tomb, and their mouths were filled with joy when they said: The Lord is risen!

*Sticheron:*

Let the nations and peoples praise Christ our God, Who willingly endured the Crucifixion for our sake and remained three days in Hades; and let them worship His three-day Resurrection from the dead, whereby the ends of the whole world were enlightened.

*Sticheron:*

Thou wast crucified and buried, O Christ, as Thou didst will; Thou didst despoil death and arise in glory as God and Master, granting unto the world eternal life (fol. 36)[^54] and great mercy!

*Other Stichera [Anatolian]:*

Truly, O lawless ones, by sealing the stone ye deemed us worthy of a greater miracle: the guards have knowledge of it; today He came forth from the tomb, and they said: Say that while we slept the disciples came and stole Him away. But who steals a dead body, especially one that is naked? He arose by His own power as God, leaving His burial wrappings in the tomb. Come and see, ye Jews, how He did not break the seals: He Who trampled down death and granted unending life and great mercy unto the human race!

*On the Beatitudes, Tone 2:*

We bring unto Thee the voice of the thief, and we pray: Remember me, O Lord, in Thy Kingdom!

*Troparion:*

We bring Thy Cross for the forgiveness of sins, which Thou didst accept for our sake, O Lover of mankind!

*Troparion:*

We worship Thy burial and arising, O Master, through which Thou didst deliver from corruption [p41LA] and the world, O Lover of mankind!

*Troparion:*

By Thy death, O Lord, Thou didst put death to death; and by Thy Resurrection, O my Savior, Thou hast enlightened the world.

*Troparion:*

Meeting the myrrh-bearing women when Thou didst arise from the tomb, Thou didst bid them proclaim Thine arising unto the disciples.

*Troparion:*

Even as Thou didst raise Adam from the tomb, O Exceedingly Good One, so, as God, by the Tree Thou didst save the world.

*Glory:*

Let us glorify the Father, let us worship the Son, and let all of us, the faithful, hymn the Holy Spirit.

*Both now. Theotokion:*

Rejoice, O Fiery-Throne; rejoice, O Mother unwedded; rejoice, O Virgin who didst bear God unto men!

---

# TONE 3 (fol. 37) [^55]

## SATURDAY EVENING: VESPERS (TONE 3)

*On Saturday Evening at Great Vespers, on "Lord, I have cried," Stichera, Tone 3:*

By Thy Cross, O Christ Savior, the dominion of death was overthrown and the deception of the devil was brought to naught; and the human race, being saved by faith, ever brings a hymn unto Thee.

*Sticheron:*

All things were enlightened by Thy Resurrection, O Lord, and Paradise was opened once again; and all creation, praising Thee, ever brings a song unto Thee.

*Sticheron:*

I glorify the power of the Father and of the Son, and I hymn the sovereignty of the Holy Spirit: undivided, uncreated Godhead, Consubstantial Trinity, reigning unto ages of ages.

*Sticheron:*

We worship Thy precious Cross, O Christ, and we hymn and glorify Thy Resurrection; for by Thy wound we all were healed.

[p42LA] *Sticheron:*

We hymn the Savior Who was incarnate of the Virgin; for our sake He was crucified, and on the third day He arose, granting us great mercy.

*Sticheron:*

Having descended unto those in Hades, Christ proclaimed glad tidings, saying: Take courage; now have I conquered! I am the Resurrection; I lead you forth, having shattered the gates of death.

*Sticheron:*

Standing unworthily in Thy most honorable house, we send up an evening hymn, crying out from the depth, O Christ God, Who hast enlightened the world by Thy three-day Resurrection: snatch Thy people from the hand of the enemies, O Lover of mankind!

*Glory, both now. Theotokion:*

How should we not marvel at thy theandric childbearing, O Most Pure Lady? For without having accepted the trial of man, O All-Blameless One, thou didst give birth in the flesh without a father unto the Son Who was born without a mother of the Father before the ages, in no way undergoing change, or confusion, or division, but preserving intact the property of each nature. Therefore, O Virgin Mother, Sovereign Lady, entreat Him that our souls may be saved, who in orthodox manner confess thee to be the Mother of God.

*At the Aposticha, Stichera of the Resurrection, Tone 3:* (fol. 38)[^56]

O Christ, Who by Thy Passion didst darken the sun, and by the light of Thy Resurrection didst enlighten all things: accept our evening hymn, O Lover of mankind!

*Verse: The Lord is King, He is robed in majesty.*

Thy life-giving arising, O Lord, enlightened the whole universe, and summoned His corrupted creation. Therefore, being delivered from the curse of Adam, we all cry aloud: O Almighty Lord, glory to Thee!

*Verse: For He hath established the world, so that it cannot be moved.*

Being God unchangeable, suffering in the flesh Thou wast changed; Whom creation, not enduring to behold hanging, was bowed down with fear, and groaning, it hymned Thy long-suffering. Having descended into Hades, Thou didst arise on the third day, granting life unto the world and great mercy.

*Verse: Holiness becomes Thine house, O Lord, unto length of days.*

That Thou mightest deliver our race from death, O Christ, Thou didst endure death; and having arisen on the third day from the dead, Thou didst raise up with Thyself those who acknowledge Thee as God; and Thou didst enlighten the world: Lord, glory to Thee!

[p43LA] *Glory, both now. Theotokion:*

Without seed from the Divine Spirit, and by the Father's good pleasure, thou didst conceive the Son of God, Who was of the Father without a mother before the ages; for our sake and of thee, having come to be without a father, thou didst bear Him in the flesh, and thou didst nourish the Infant with milk. Therefore cease not to pray that our souls may be delivered from tribulations.

*Now Thou dismissest. Holy God. After Our Father:*

*Troparion, Tone 3:*

Let the heavens be glad, and let the earthly rejoice; for the Lord hath wrought might with His arm: He trampled down death by death, He became the Firstborn of the dead, He delivered us from the belly of Hades, and granted great mercy unto the world!

*Glory, both now. Theotokion:*

Thee, who didst mediate the salvation of our race, we hymn, O Virgin Mother of God; for having accepted the Passion of the Cross in the flesh taken from thee, Thy Son and our God delivered us from corruption, as Lover of mankind.

## SUNDAY MIDNIGHT OFFICE (TONE 3)

*At the Midnight Office, Canon to the Holy Trinity. Ode 1, Tone 3. Heirmos: He Who of old by divine beckoning...* (fol. 39)[^57]

*Troparion:*

Incomprehensible, Sole Sovereign, and One Tri-hypostatic Godhead: deem me worthy of Thy Tri-radiant Light, that I may hymn Thee Who art unceasingly sung with thrice-holy hymns by angelic mouths!

*Troparion:*

All the immaterial ranks sacredly sing of Thee as the sole Tri-radiant Creative Cause, the Supreme Light; Which human multitudes also harmoniously sing of, and faithfully glorify with earthen mouths.

*Glory:*

The theologians fittingly and compositely call Thee Father, and Word, and Spirit: designating the Son impassibly from the Unbegotten Father, and together the procession of the Divine Spirit, O God, Sole Sovereign over all!

*Both now. Theotokion:*

As Lover of mankind by nature, taking human substance [p44LA] having assumed human nature, O Divine Word, Thou didst shine forth as the Tri-hypostatic, Supreme-Sovereign Light, having shown forth the undivided Godhead, exceedingly glorified unto all, through the Pure Virgin who bore Thee.

*Ode 3. Heirmos: He Who brought all things out of nothing...*

Bidding Elijah of old to arrange the water upon the logs, He figuratively revealed unto all the Tri-hypostatic constitution of the Sole Godhead's Dominion.

*Troparion:*

The corruptible nature of earth-born mortals hymns Thee, the One and Tri-radiant, unchangeable Creator; and I cry unto Thee, O Master: Deliver me from every sudden destruction, and save me!

*Glory:*

Clearly proclaiming through prophetic words, and through the all-wise Apostles, preachers of the faith: we, the faithful, glorify Thee, the Equally-Effective Trinity, O God of all!

*Both now. Theotokion:*

From the high Throne the Word came down to make man higher, as Lover of mankind, through thee, O Most Pure Lady; and the Tri-solar Light shone forth upon all.

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 3:*

O Undivided and Inseparable Trinity, have mercy upon us who worship Thee, and who piously hymn and unceasingly call upon Thee: save those whom Thou hast gathered together in Thy holy Church because of mercy, O Mother, to glorify Thy dominion; for as Thou art merciful, Thou knowest the weakness of our heart (fol. 40).[^58]

*Glory, both now. Theotokion:*

I am completely changed through mine intemperance, and through my fearlessness, foolish, senseless, accursed in lack of feeling: I tremble not before the day at which all things visible and invisible tremble. O Lady, help me and destroy the perplexity of my heart; for thou thyself art the hope and protectress unto thy servants!

*Ode 4. Heirmos: Thou hast set firm love toward us: the...*

*Troparion:*

A two-fold branch sprang forth from the Father as from a root: the upright Son and the Spirit, consubstantial offshoots, divinely planted and co-unoriginate flowers, so that the Godhead is of Three Lights.

*Troparion:*

The assemblies of the noetic beings unceasingly hymn Thee, the Inscrutable God; with whom we also glorify Thee, saying: O Supremely Essential Trinity, save [p45LA] Thy servants, as Lover of mankind!

*Both now. Theotokion:*

Thou didst inflame us with Thy love, O Abundantly Merciful Word of God, Who for our sake wast incarnate without change, and mystically didst declare the Tri-radiant One Godhead; therefore we glorify Thee!

*Ode 5. Heirmos: As Isaiah figuratively beheld upon a throne...*

*Troparion:*

As Isaiah figuratively beheld God, the One Sovereign, glorified in Three Persons with most honorable voices, a Seraph was straightway sent to proclaim the Tri-radiant Essence and the Tri-solar Monad.

*Glory:*

O Tri-solar Monad, Who didst establish the nature of all things invisible and visible out of non-being before: deliver from all temptations those who faithfully hymn Thee as the One God, and deem them worthy of Thy glory!

*Both now. Theotokion:*

Having become the light-bearing and honorable bridal-chamber unto God, O Virgin, we hymn thee with love and bless thee; for of thee Christ was born in two natures, with dual wills, Who is One of the Trinity and the Lord existing in glory!

*Ode 6. Heirmos: The uttermost abyss of sins hath encompassed me...* (fol. 41)[^59]

*Troparion:*

Being a sojourner, Abraham was deemed worthy to receive figuratively, in the likeness of the Lord, the Supremely Essential One in Three Hypostases, under the forms of men.

*Glory:*

Direct the hearts of Thy servants unto the Unapproachable Light, O Tri-solar Lord, and grant the radiance of Thy glory unto our souls, that we may be illumined by Thine ineffable beauty!

*Both now. Theotokion:*

Open unto me the gates of the Light Who was born of thy womb, O Most Pure Lady, that I may behold the Tri-radiant beam of the Godhead, and glorify thee, the All-Radiant Lady!

*Kontakion, Tone 3:*

When Thou comest, O Master, upon the earth to judge all whom Thou didst fashion from the earth according to Thine image: have compassion as Merciful One, and deliver all from Thy righteous judgment, and send us not into the fire of Gehenna, as Thou alone art Christ, the Abundantly Merciful!

*Ikos:*

Groaning [p46LA] Grant me groaning, O Virgin, even as unto the Publican, and tears like the harlot, that offering these unto thee I may cry aloud: Have mercy on me, O All-Merciful Lady, and destroy my perplexity by thy grace, pouring down upon me a rain of compunction; for unto thee have I fled, O Most Pure Lady!

*Ode 7. Heirmos: As of old Thou didst bedew the three...*

*Troparion:*

Show me forth as a temple of Thy Tri-radiant, all-luminous Godhead, O Master, higher than grievous sinful darkness and passions! By Thy light-giving radiances: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Troparion:*

We proclaim one appearance of Thy Godhead in Three Hypostatic and distinct properties: Father, Son, and Spirit, crying: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Glory:*

God appeared unto Abraham in three hypostases of old by the Oak of Mamre, granting Isaac as reward for hospitality through mercy; Whom we also now glorify as the God of our fathers.

*Both now. Theotokion:*

The Creator appeared on earth, having become man in God-befitting manner from thy virginal and most pure womb, and He renewed us: O blessed, most pure Mother of God, All-Pure Lady! (fol. 42)[^60]

*Ode 8. Heirmos: Gathered together in the unenduring fire, yet unharmed...*

*Troparion:*

From the Unoriginate Father, as from a root, the Word and the Spirit, co-unoriginate existing by nature, as offshoots of the Supremely Essential Godhead, showed forth the one glory and power of the Trinity; Which all of us, the faithful, hymn unto the ages!

*Troparion:*

By Thy splendors Thou directest the heavenly ranks to hymn unceasingly with thrice-holy divine songs: O Father, Word of the same form, and Spirit, the Tri-radiant and equally-potent dominion; therefore we hymn Thee unto the ages.

*Both now. Theotokion:*

Beholding thy childbearing from afar, the prophetic utterances praised it, O Mother of God, as having been born without seed and beyond nature, O Lady; and harmoniously we hymn Him as King, and exceedingly exalt Him unto all ages!

*Ode 9. Heirmos: A new wonder and God-befitting: for through the...*

*Troparion:*

Manifestly desiring to behold the Tri-radiant glory, the God-beholding ranks of the bodiless hosts with immaterial wings [p47LA] desire to ascend on high; but they veil themselves exceedingly with the unapproachable Light, and unceasingly cry out a hymn; with them harmoniously, O Sole Trinity, we glorify Thee!

*Troparion:*

With insatiable love, we who are upon earth, having received from Thee a noetic and rational soul, hymn Thee as God the Almighty, the Sole Essence in truth, and Tri-hypostatic Persons from the whole heart; therefore, O Compassionate One, as Abundantly Merciful, have compassion upon us!

*Glory, both now. Theotokion:*

Show me forth as a light-bearing temple of Thy Sole Sovereign and Tri-radiant Godhead: cleanly to serve Thee, the Creator of all, and noetically to behold Thine ineffable glory, through the prayers of the only Mother of God, whom worthily, as exceedingly glorious, we magnify!

*After this we sing: It is truly meet. [End of Midnight Office]*

## SUNDAY MATINS (TONE 3)

*On Sunday at Matins, after "God is the Lord": on 8: Tone 3. Troparion: Let the heavens be glad... (see above). Glory, both now. Theotokion.*

*After the First Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 3:* (fol. 43)[^61]

Christ is risen from the dead, the First-fruits of those who have fallen asleep: the Firstborn of creation and the Maker of all things that have come to be; and He renewed in Himself the corrupted nature of our race. No longer dost thou have dominion, O death; for the Master of all hath overthrown thy power!

*Glory:*

Adoring the unchangeable Godhead and Thy voluntary Passion, O Lord, Hades, terrified within himself, lamented, saying: I tremble at the flesh of incorruptible structure; I behold the Invisible One mystically warring against me! Therefore those whom I hold also cry aloud: Glory, O Christ, to Thy Resurrection!

*Both now. Theotokion:*

The heavenly beings rejoiced with love, and the earthly were terrified with trembling, when the most pure voice came upon thee, O Mother of God; for one triumph shone forth upon both when the Bodiless One brought joy unto thee. Therefore with the Angel we cry unto thee: Rejoice, O Pure Lady, Dwelling-place of God!

*After the Second Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 3:*

Having tasted death in the flesh, O Lord, by His arising He cut short the bitterness of death, and strengthened man again, recalling the overcoming of the first curse [p48LA] O Defender of our life, Christ God, glory to Thee!

*Glory:*

We, the faithful, praise the incomprehensible Crucifixion and ineffable Resurrection, an unutterable mystery; for today death and Hades were despoiled, and the human race was robed in incorruption. Therefore also thankfully we cry aloud: Glory, O Christ, to Thy Resurrection!

*Both now. Theotokion:*

The Incomprehensible and Uncircumscribed One, consubstantial with the Father and the Spirit, thou didst mystically contain within thy womb, O Mother of God; we have come to know one and unconfused Godhead of the Trinity, to glorify thy childbearing in the world. Therefore also thankfully we cry unto thee: Rejoice, O Full of Grace!

*Hypakoe, Tone 3:*

Astonishing by his appearance, refreshing with words, the shining Angel said unto the myrrh-bearing women: Why seek ye the Living One in the tomb? He is risen, Who emptied the tombs! Understand the unchangeable Changer of corruption; say unto God: How awesome are Thy deeds, for Thou hast saved the human race!

*Antiphon 1, Tone 3:* (fol. 44)[^62]

Thou didst deliver the captivity of Zion from Babylon; draw me also from passions unto life, O Word!

*Verse:*

They that sow in the South with divine tears shall reap sheaves of ever-living joy.

*Glory, both now:*

Unto the Holy Spirit belongs every thanksgiving: even as unto the Father and the Son, He co-shines; in Him all things live and move.

*Antiphon 2:*

Unless the Lord build the house of virtues, in vain do we labor; and if He protect the soul, no one shall overthrow our city.

*Verse:*

Through the Spirit, the saints are ever adopted sons of the fruit of the womb unto Thee, O Christ, even as unto the Father.

*Glory, both now:*

Through the Holy Spirit all holiness and wisdom are foreseen; for He gives essence unto every creature. Let us serve Him, for He is God, even as the Father and the Word!

*Antiphon 3:*

Blessed are they that fear the Lord, walking in the way of the commandments; for they shall eat of all the fruit that gives life.

[p49LA] *Verse:*

Be glad around thy table, beholding thy offspring bearing branches of good deeds unto the Chief Shepherd!

*Glory, both now:*

Through the Holy Spirit comes every richness of glory; from Him is grace and life unto every creature; for He is hymned together with the Father and with the Word.

*Prokeimenon, Tone 3:*

Say among the heathen that the Lord reigns; for He hath established the world, which shall not be moved.

*Verse: O sing unto the Lord a new song.*

*Let every breath praise the Lord. Verse: Praise God in His saints.*

*Sunday Matins Gospel. Having beheld the Resurrection of Christ.*

*Canon of the Resurrection. Ode 1, Tone 3. Heirmos: He Who of old by divine beckoning gathered the waters into one gathering...*

He Who of old by divine beckoning gathered the waters into one gathering, and divided the sea for the people of Israel: this is our God, exceedingly glorified; unto Him alone let us sing, for He hath been glorified!

*Troparion:*

He Who condemned the earth, unto the transgressor afterward to bring forth the fruit of thorns: this is our God, Who received in the flesh a crown of thorns from the hands of the lawless; He dissolved the curse, for He hath been glorified! (fol. 45)[^63]

*Troparion:*

The Victor and Vanquisher of death, having appeared as One Who feared death; for having assumed animate, passible flesh, this is our God; and having wrestled with the tormentor, He resurrected all with Himself, for He hath been glorified!

*Theotokion:*

All nations glorify thee as the true Mother of God, who didst bear without seed; for having descended into thy sanctified womb, this is our God, Who was substantialized for our sake, and was born of thee as God and Man!

*Another Canon, of the Cross and Resurrection. Tone 3. Heirmos: The same:*

Let us, the people, sing a new song unto God Who wrought: the human race, enslaved by the sin-loving tormentor, Christ redeemed with His Divine Blood, and having deified it, He renewed it, for He hath been glorified!

*Troparion:*

Even as mortal, Christ willed to taste of death, He Who is the Treasury of life; and being immortal by nature, He brought the dead to life, for He hath been glorified!

*Another Canon, to the Most Holy Theotokos. Heirmos: [The same]*

[p50LA] *Theotokion:*

Every nature of heavenly beings bends the knee unto Him Who was incarnate of thee, O Virgin, and together with earthly beings, fittingly those under the earth, for He hath been glorified!

*Theotokion:*

At the reconciliation that was toward thee: for as God, the begrudgeless Giver of good things, having given the Divine Spirit unto us, He assumed flesh of thee, O Maiden, for He hath been glorified!

*Ode 3. Heirmos: He Who brought all things out of nothing...*

He Who brought all things out of non-being by the Word, which are fashioned and perfected by the Spirit: O Almighty Master, establish me in Thy love!

*Troparion:*

By Thy Cross the ungodly one was put to shame; for having made a pit which he dug, he fell into it; but in Thine arising, O Christ, Thou didst raise the horn of the humble.

*Troparion:*

The proclamation of godliness unto the nations covered the sea like water, O Lover of mankind; for having arisen from the tomb, Thou didst reveal the Tri-hypostatic Light!

*Theotokion:*

Glorious things were spoken of thee, O animate City of Him Who reigns ever; for through thee, O Lady, God dwelt with those on earth!

*Another Heirmos [Cross-Resurrection]:* (fol. 46)[^64]

...Thou didst crush the [strength] of the enemies; the shield Thou didst wipe away by Thy might, O Master Christ, our confirmation: Holy art Thou, O Lord!

*Troparion:*

Thou, O Most Pure Cross, hast been shown forth as purity against idolatrous impurities; for Jesus, the Supremely Divine, stretched forth His hands upon thee!

*Troparion:*

That all of us, the faithful, may worship Thee, O Life-Giving Tomb; for Christ, truly our God, was buried in Thee and arose!

*Another Canon, to the Most Holy Theotokos. Heirmos:*

A Rod from the root of Jesse, sprouting forth prophetically, O Virgin: Christ blossomed forth as a flower for us from thee: Holy art Thou, O Lord!

*Troparion:*

That Thou mightest make earth-born mortals partakers of the Divine Nature: Thou, having become poor, didst assume our flesh from the Virgin: Holy art Thou, O Lord!

*Ode 4. Heirmos: Thou hast set firm love toward us: the...*

Thou hast placed steadfast love toward us, O Lord; for for our sake Thou didst give Thine Only-Begotten Son unto death; therefore we cry unto Thee thankfully: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!

*Troparion:*

Thou didst mercifully accept wounds and stripes, O Christ, enduring the outrage of being struck on the cheeks, and patiently bearing spitting, through which [p51LA] whereby Thou didst effect salvation for me: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!

*Troparion:*

Having a mortal body, Thou, the Life, didst partake of death: for the sake of the passion of the poor and the groaning of the needy; and having corrupted the corruptor, O Supremely Glorified One, Thou didst raise up all with Thyself, for Thou art glorified!

*Troparion:*

Remember, O Christ, the flock which Thou didst acquire by Thy Passion: accepting the prayerful entreaties of Thy supremely glorified Mother; and having visited the afflicted, deliver it by Thy power, O Lord!

*Another Heirmos [Cross-Resurrection]: Strange and ineffable is Thy birth...*

*Troparion:*

O Lover of mankind, Who didst fashion man according to Thine own image: when he was put to death on account of the sin of transgression, being crucified upon the Place of the Skull, Thou didst save him.

*Troparion:*

The dead whom death had devoured it surrendered, and Thou didst destroy the corrupting kingdom of Hades when Thou didst arise from the tomb, O Lord.

*Theotokion:*

O Pure Mary, Golden Censer: without composition, as One of the Trinity having descended into thee, God the Word, having become incarnate, filled the world with sweet fragrance.

*Another Heirmos [to the Most Holy Theotokos]:*

O Master Who didst set the mountains by the measure of divine wisdom: Thou, the Stone cut without hands from the Virgin, [didst appear]: Glory to Thy power, O Lover of mankind!

*Troparion:* (fol. 47)[^65]

Thou didst heal our diseased nature, O Master, having joined whole that which was grieving [taken] from the Virgin unto Thy precious Divinity, O Word.

*Troparion:*

Thou art my portion, O Lord, and desired inheritance, O Word, Who hast joined me from the Virgin unto Thine own Hypostasis, having become substantial in the flesh.

*Ode 5. Heirmos: Unto Thee do I rise early in the...*

Unto Thee I rise early in the morning, O Creator of all, which surpasses every mind of the world; for Thy commandments are light: guide me therein.

*Troparion:*

The All-Seeing One and He Who judges all the earth righteously, being delivered unto an unrighteous judge by Hebrew envy, Thou didst deliver ancient Adam from condemnation.

*Troparion:*

Grant Thy peace unto Thy churches, O Christ, by the unconquerable power of Thy Cross, O Thou Who didst arise from the dead, and save our souls.

*Theotokion:*

O Holy Tabernacle and more spacious than the heavens, as having received the everywhere-incomprehensible Creator, the Word of God, thou alone wast shown forth, O Ever-Virgin.

*Another Heirmos [Cross-Resurrection]: He Who was invisible on earth was seen...*

*Troparion:*

Pierced in Thy side by a spear, O my Christ [p52LA] having been pierced, Thou didst deliver from the curse her that was created from man's rib, who had become the mediatress of destruction unto all men.

*Troparion:*

Being equal to the Father in essence, O Christ our Savior, Thou didst raise from the dead the sacred temple of Thine immaculate and all-honorable Body.

*Theotokion:*

The Word of God, Thy Son, O Virgin, the Word of God, is the Creator of first-formed Adam, uncreated; even though He created for Himself an animate flesh from thee.

*Theotokion:*

Equal to the Father is Thy Son, O Virgin, the Word of God: a perfect Hypostasis in two natures, Jesus, Lord and God, perfect [God] and man.

*Ode 6. Heirmos: The uttermost abyss of sins hath surrounded me...*

The uttermost abyss of sins hath surrounded me, and my spirit fails; but having stretched forth Thine exalted arm, O Master, save me, O Pilot, as Thou didst Peter.

*Troparion:*

An abyss of mercy and compassions hath surrounded me by Thy compassionate condescension; for having become incarnate, O Master, and having been in the form of a servant, Thou didst deify it, and didst glorify it with Thyself.

*Troparion:*

The slayer suffered slaying when he beheld him that was slain made alive by Thy Resurrection...

*Troparion:* (fol. 48)[^66]

...Resurrection: these, O Christ, are the tokens and the victories of Thine immaculate Passions.

*Theotokion:*

O Most Pure Lady, who alone beyond understanding didst mediate between the Creator and men: pray Thy Son that He may be merciful unto Thy sinful servants, and a champion.

*Another Heirmos [Cross-Resurrection]: The dwelling of Jonah in the nethermost parts of Hades...*

The dwelling of Jonah in the nethermost parts of Hades was a figurative image: bring up my life from corruption, O Lover of mankind.

*Troparion:*

Having been tried by Thy wounds, wounded by Hades, Thou didst raise him up by the Passion of the Cross; therefore I cry unto Thee: Bring up my life from corruption, O Lover of mankind!

*Troparion:*

The gates of Hades open unto Thee in fear, O Christ, and the vessels of the enemy are plundered; therefore the women met Thee, receiving joy instead of sorrow.

*Another Heirmos [to the Most Holy Theotokos]:*

He Who is inseparable in form is conformed unto that which is ours from the incorrupt Virgin; having become [man] in form and in matter, He was not changed in His Divinity, [becoming] man.

*Theotokion:*

From the abysses of sins and the tempest of passions, O Most Pure Lady, deliver me; for thou art a harbor and an abyss of wonders unto those who hasten unto thee with faith.

*Kontakion, Tone 3:*

[p53LA] *Kontakion, Tone 3:*

Thou didst arise today from the tomb, O Merciful One, and didst lead us forth from the gates of death; today Adam dances and Eve rejoices, and together the Prophets with the Patriarchs unceasingly hymn the divine dominion of Thy might.

*Ikos:*

Let heaven and earth dance today, and let them hymn Christ God with one mind, for He hath raised the prisoners from the tombs; all creation rejoices together, offering worthy hymns unto the Creator of all and our Redeemer, for having today led men forth from Hades as the Giver of life, He carried them up to heaven with Himself, and cast down the haughty liftings of the enemy, and smashes the gates of Hades by the divine dominion of His power.

*Ode 7. Heirmos: As of old Thou didst bedew the three...*

As of old Thou didst refresh the three pious youths in the Chaldean flame, illuminating them with the bright fire of the Godhead, as they cried: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Troparion:*

The bright temple veil was torn at the crucifixion of the Creator, revealing unto the faithful the truth hidden in the Scripture, as they cry: Blessed art Thou...

*Troparion:* (fol. 49)[^67]

...crying: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Troparion:*

When Thy side was pierced, O Christ, a drop of the God-flowing, life-creating Blood, dripping down upon the earth by Thy dispensation, renewed those who are from the earth, as they cry: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Troparion:*

Let us glorify the Good Spirit together with the Father, and with the Only-Begotten Son, we the faithful honoring one Principle and one Godhead in Three [Persons], crying: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Another Heirmos [Cross-Resurrection]: The proud tormentor became a plaything unto the youths...*

The proud tormentor became a plaything unto the youths; for trampling upon the sevenfold flame like dust, they sang: Blessed art Thou, O Lord, God of our fathers!

*Troparion:*

Seeing not simply a man hanging upon the Cross, but God incarnate, the sun was darkened; unto Whom we sing: Blessed art Thou, O Lord, God of our fathers!

*Troparion:*

Having received Him Who is mighty in Godhead, the Giver of incorruption, fearful Hades vomited forth the souls of the righteous, who cry: Blessed art Thou, O Lord, God of our fathers!

*Theotokion:*

Thou wast shown forth as a priceless treasure of blessing, O Most Pure Lady, unto those who with pure heart confess thee to be the Mother of God; for of thee was incarnate the God of our fathers.

*Another Heirmos [to the Most Holy Theotokos]:*

[p54LA] ...The Lord of glory, Who holds dominion over the heavenly hosts, Who sits with the Father, is carried by virginal hands: Blessed art Thou, O Lord, God of our fathers!

*Troparion:*

Death reigned; but partaking of it from the Virgin, Thou didst destroy it, having become God-composed flesh: Blessed art Thou, O Lord, God of our fathers!

*Theotokion:*

We all have acknowledged thee who didst bear God to be the Mother of God; for thou didst bear of thee One of the Trinity Who was incarnate: Blessed, O Most Pure Lady, is the fruit of thy womb!

*Ode 8. Heirmos: Gathered together in the unenduring fire, yet unharmed...*

United in the unendurable fire, the youths who stood for piety, unharmed by the flame, sang the divine hymn: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord; sing and exalt Him supremely unto all ages!

*Troparion:*

The bright splendor of the temple was rent when Thy Cross was planted on the Place of the Skull, and creation bowed down in fear, singing: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord; sing and exalt Him supremely unto all ages!

*Troparion:*

Thou didst arise from the tomb, O Christ, and by divine power Thou didst set upright him who had fallen by the deception of the tree, as they cry and say: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord; sing and exalt Him supremely unto all ages!

*Theotokion:* (fol. 50)[^68]

Thou wast revealed as the Temple of God, an animate dwelling and Ark; for, O Mother of God, Most Pure Lady, thou didst reconcile the Creator unto men; and worthily, all ye works, we hymn and exalt thee supremely unto all ages!

*Another Heirmos [Cross-Resurrection]: The God-bearing youths extinguished the material fire's flame...*

The God-bearing youths extinguished the material fire's flame in immaterial manner, and sang: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord, and exalt Him supremely unto ages!

*Troparion:*

The impassible Word, impassible indeed in Godhead, yet God suffers in the flesh; unto Whom also we sing: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord, sing and exalt Him supremely unto ages!

*Troparion:*

Thou didst fall asleep as mortal, and didst arise as immortal, O Savior, and Thou dost save from death those who sing: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord, sing and exalt Him supremely unto ages!

*Troparion:*

Let us piously serve the Tri-hypostatic Godhead, united ineffably, and let us sing: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord, sing and exalt Him supremely unto ages!

*Another Heirmos [to the Most Holy Theotokos]:*

As Mother, thou didst surpass the noetic ranks [p55LA] and having come near unto God: We bless thy childbearing, O Blessed Virgin, and exalt [thee] supremely unto all ages!

*Troparion:*

Thou didst show forth natural beauty most excellent, flesh radiant with Godhead: We bless thy childbearing, O Blessed Virgin, and exalt [thee] supremely unto all ages!

*Ode 9. Heirmos: A new wonder and God-befitting: for through the...*

A new and divinely fitting wonder: the virginal door being shut, the Lord manifestly passes through; naked at His entering, and God appeared bearing flesh at His coming forth, and the door remains shut: her unceasingly as Mother of God we magnify.

*Troparion:*

Awesome it is to behold Thee, the Creator, lifted up upon the Tree, O Word of God: God suffering in the flesh for His servants, and lying breathless in the tomb, yet releasing the dead from Hades; therefore, O Christ, as Almighty we magnify Thee.

*Troparion:*

From mortal corruption Thou didst save the Forefather, O Christ, being laid dead in the tomb; and Thou didst cause life to blossom forth, having raised the dead; by Thy hand Thou didst lead corrupt nature unto the light, clothing it in divine incorruption; therefore Thee, the Light of the Ever-Living Spring, we magnify.

*Theotokion:* (fol. 51)[^69]

A receptacle of Light did He show thee forth, O Pure One: God, Who was ineffably and unchangeably incarnate of thee, having lifted up Adam's essence upon the Cross, having become the Firstborn from the dead; and He abides in the highest, together glorified with the Father and the Divine Spirit.

*Another Heirmos [Cross-Resurrection]: Wounded by the sweet arrow of thy most pure childbearing, O Pure One...*

Wounded by the sweet arrow of thy most pure childbearing, O Pure One, and marveling at thy most amiable beauty, with angelic hymns worthily as Mother of God we magnify thee.

*Troparion:*

Honor unto men, [delivering] all from ignominious death, Thou didst pour forth, having tasted it by Thy crucifixion, O Savior; unto me, mortal by nature, as God Thou didst grant incorruption, O Lover of mankind.

*Troparion:*

Thou didst save me, having arisen from the tomb, O Christ, and from torment, and didst lead me unto Thy Father and Begetter, and didst seat me at His right hand: O tender compassion of Thy mercy, O Lord!

*Another Heirmos [to the Most Holy Theotokos], the same:*

Full satiation of thy praises, O Virgin, can never be for the pious faithful; for receiving desire upon desire, ever divine and spiritual, as Mother of God we magnify thee.

[p56LA] *Troparion:*

Thou hast given us an unashamed intercessor, her who bore Thee, O Christ; by her entreaties, being merciful, Thou dost give us the Spirit, the Giver of grace, proceeding from the Father through Thee.

*Resurrectional Exapostilarion.*

*At the Praises, Stichera of the Resurrection, Tone 3:*

Come, all ye nations, understand the power of the awesome mystery: for Christ our Savior, the Word Who was in the beginning, was crucified for our sake, and willingly was buried, and arose from the dead to save all things: let us worship Him!

*Sticheron:*

Thy guards recounted all the wonders, O Lord; but the council of vanity filled their right hands with a bribe, thinking to conceal Thy Resurrection, which the world glorifies: Have mercy on us!

*Sticheron:*

All things were filled with joy, having received the proof of the Resurrection; for Mary Magdalene came unto the tomb, and found an Angel sitting upon the stone, shining in his raiment and saying: Why seek ye the Living among the dead? He is not here, but is risen, as He said, going before you into Galilee!

*Sticheron:* (fol. 52)[^70]

In Thy light, O Master, we shall see light, O Lover of mankind; for Thou didst arise from the dead, granting salvation unto the human race, that all creation may hymn Thee, the only Sinless One: Have mercy on us!

*Eastern Stichera, Tone 3:*

*Sticheron:*

The Myrrh-bearing women brought a morning hymn with tears, O Lord; for having sweet-smelling fragrances, they reached Thy tomb, striving to anoint Thine immaculate Body; but the Angel sitting upon the stone brought them good tidings: Why seek ye the Living among the dead? For having despoiled death, He arose as God, granting great mercy unto all!

*Sticheron:*

An Angel shining upon Thy life-giving tomb said unto the Myrrh-bearing women: The Redeemer has emptied the tombs, has overthrown Hades, and arose on the third day, as the only God and Almighty!

*Verse: Arise, O Lord my God, let Thy hand be lifted up; forget not Thy poor unto the end.*

*Sticheron:*

Coming unto the tomb to seek Thee on the first day of the week, Mary Magdalene, not having found [Thee] [p57LA] grieving with weeping, cried aloud: Woe is me, O my Savior! He has been stolen away, O King of all! And a pair of life-bearing Angels within the tomb cried aloud: Why weepest thou, O woman? I weep, she said, because they have taken away my Lord from the tomb, and I know not where they have laid Him. And turning back, when she beheld Thee, she immediately cried out: My Lord and my God, glory to Thee!

*Verse: I will give thanks unto Thee, O Lord, with my whole heart; I will speak of all Thy marvelous works.*

*Sticheron:*

The Jews shut up Life in the tomb, but the thief opened Paradise with his tongue, crying out and saying: He Who is crucified with me and for my sake hung with me upon the Tree, and is revealed sitting upon the Throne with the Father: He is Christ our Savior, having great mercy!

*Glory: Evangelical Morning Sticheron. Both now. Theotokion: Most blessed art thou...*

*At the Liturgy, Beatitudes, Tone 3:*

*Troparion:*

Adam our forefather, who transgressed Thy commandment, O Christ, Thou didst cast out of Paradise; but the thief, O Merciful One, who confessed Thee upon the Cross, Thou didst settle therein, as he cried: Remember me, O Savior, in Thy Kingdom!

*Troparion:* (fol. 53)[^71]

...mercifully, who confessed Thee upon the Cross, Thou didst settle therein, as he cried: Remember me, O Savior, in Thy Kingdom!

*Troparion:*

When Thou wast crucified, O Christ, all creation was changed, seeing its Master in human nature; therefore the thief cried out unto Thee: Remember me when Thou comest in Thy Kingdom!

*Troparion:*

When we had sinned, Thou didst condemn us with the curse of death, O Giver of life and Lord; yet in Thy Body, O Sinless Master, having suffered, Thou didst make the dead man alive, as he cries: Remember us also in Thy Kingdom!

*Troparion:*

Having arisen from the dead, Thou didst raise us from passions by Thy Resurrection, O Lord, and Thou didst destroy all the power of death, O Savior; therefore with faith we cry unto Thee: Remember us also in Thy Kingdom!

*Troparion:*

Unto the Myrrh-bearing women Thou didst first appear, having arisen from the dead, O Savior, crying aloud: Rejoice! and through them [didst bid them] announce Thine arising unto Thy friends, O Christ; therefore: Remember us also in Thy Kingdom!

*Troparion:*

On the mountain Moses, having stretched out his hands, prefigured the Cross, overcoming Amalek; [do Thou, O Christ Savior, raise us up who have been cast down by his snares, as] [p58LA] we faithfully cry: Remember us also in Thy Kingdom!

*Glory. Troas [Trinity Hymn]:*

Unto the Father, the Son, and the Holy Spirit let us, the faithful, bow down: the uncreated Trinity, in three Persons and in one Godhead, Who is ever faithfully glorified by the Bodiless Hosts in thrice-holy sanctifications.

*Both now. Theotokion:*

Rejoice, O Door of God, through which the Creator passed, having become incarnate, preserving thee sealed! Rejoice, O Light Cloud, carrying Christ, the Divine Rain! Rejoice, O Ladder and Heavenly Throne! Rejoice, O Mountain of God, honorable, fertile, and unquarried!

---

# TONE 4 (fol. 54) [^72]

## SATURDAY EVENING: VESPERS (TONE 4)

*On Saturday Evening at Great Vespers, on "Lord, I have cried," Stichera of the Resurrection, Tone 4:*

*Sticheron:*

Ceaselessly bowing down before Thy life-giving Cross, O Christ, we glorify Thy three-day Resurrection; for thereby, O Almighty One, Thou didst renew corrupted human nature, and didst renew for us the ascent into heaven, as the only Good One and Lover of mankind.

*Sticheron:*

Thou didst loose the penalty of the tree of disobedience, O Savior, being willingly nailed to the tree of the Cross; and having descended into Hades, O Mighty One, as God Thou didst burst the bonds of death asunder; therefore we worship Thine arising from the dead, crying out with joy: O Almighty Lord, glory to Thee!

*Sticheron:*

Thou didst shatter the gates of Hades, O Lord, and by Thy death Thou didst destroy the kingdom of death; and Thou didst set the human race free from corruption [p59LA] granting life and incorruption unto the world, and great mercy.

*Other Stichera, Eastern [by Anatolius]:*

*Sticheron:*

Come, O ye peoples, let us hymn the Savior's three-day arising, whereby we were delivered from the unbreakable bonds of Hades, and we all received incorruption and life, crying aloud: O Thou Who wast crucified and buried, and didst arise, save us by Thy Resurrection, O only Lover of mankind!

*Sticheron:*

Angels and men, O Savior, hymn Thy three-day arising, whereby the ends of the universe were enlightened, and we all were delivered from the servitude of the enemy, crying aloud: O Life-Creating, Almighty Savior, save us by Thy Resurrection, O only Lover of mankind!

*Sticheron:*

Thou didst shatter the brazen gates and didst crush the iron bars, O Christ God, and didst raise up fallen human race; therefore with one accord we cry unto Thee: O Thou Who didst arise from the dead, Lord, glory to Thee!

*Sticheron:*

Lord, Thy birth from the Father is timeless and co-eternal; and Thine incarnation from the Virgin is ineffable unto men and unutterable; and Thy descent into Hades is fearful unto the devil and his angels; for having trampled down death, Thou didst arise on the third day...

*Glory, both now. Theotokion:* (fol. 55)[^73]

...granting incorruption unto men and great mercy.

David, the Prophet and Forefather of God, on account of thee melodiously proclaimed of thee unto Him Who hath done magnificent things for thee: The Queen stood at Thy right hand; for God showed thee forth as Mother and Mediatress of life, Who was pleased to become man from thee without a father, that He might renew His own image corrupted by passions, and having found the lost sheep that had wandered upon the mountain, taking it upon His shoulder, He might bring it unto the Father, and by His own will unite it with the heavenly hosts, and, O Mother of God, might save the world: Christ, Who hath great and rich mercy!

*At the Aposticha, Stichera, Tone 4:*

*Sticheron:*

Ascending the Cross, O Lord, Thou didst wipe away our ancestral curse; and descending into Hades, Thou didst set free the eternal prisoners, granting incorruption unto the human race; therefore, singing, we glorify Thy life-creating and saving Resurrection.

*Other Stichera, by Alphabet, Tone 4:*

*Verse: The Lord is King, He is robed in majesty.*

*Sticheron:*

Hanging upon the Tree, O only Mighty One, all creation [p60LA] Thou didst shake, O only Mighty One; and being laid in the tomb, Thou didst raise up those dwelling in the tombs, granting incorruption and life unto the human race; therefore, singing, we glorify Thy three-day arising.

*Verse: For He hath established the world, so that it cannot be moved.*

*Sticheron:*

The lawless people, having delivered Thee unto Pilate, O Christ, condemned Thee to be crucified, showing themselves ungrateful toward their Benefactor; but willingly having endured burial by Thine own authority, Thou didst arise on the third day as God, granting us endless life and great mercy.

*Verse: Holiness becomes Thine house, O Lord, unto length of days.*

*Sticheron:*

The women with tears reached the tomb, seeking Thee; but not having found Thee, weeping with lamentation they cried out, saying: Woe is us, O Savior and King of all! How wast Thou stolen away? What place holds Thy life-bearing Body? And the Angel answered them, saying: Weep not, but go and proclaim that the Lord is risen, granting us joy, as the only Compassionate One!

*Glory, both now. Theotokion:*

Incline Thyself unto the entreaty of thy servants...

...O All-Blameless Lady, calming the grievous uprisings against us, delivering us from every affliction; for thee alone have we as a firm and sure confirmation, and we have acquired thy protection: let us not be put to shame, O Sovereign Lady, who call upon thee! Hasten unto the supplication of those who faithfully cry unto thee: Rejoice, O Sovereign Lady, Helper of all, Joy, and Protection, and Salvation of our souls!

*Dismissal Troparion of the Resurrection, Tone 4:* (fol. 56)[^74]

Having learned the joyful proclamation of the Resurrection from the Angel, and cast off the ancestral condemnation, the women disciples of the Lord boasted unto the Apostles, saying: Death has been overthrown, and Christ God is risen, granting great mercy unto the world!

*Glory, both now. Theotokion:*

The mystery hidden from the ages and unknown unto the Angels was revealed unto those on earth through thee, O Mother of God: God being incarnate in unconfused union, and willingly accepting the Cross for our sake, whereby having raised the first-formed man, He saved our souls from death.

## SUNDAY MIDNIGHT OFFICE (TONE 4)

[p61LA] *On Sunday at the Midnight Office, Canon to the Most Holy and Life-Originating Trinity, the work of the most honorable Gregory; the fourth song [Tone 4], the work of Mitrophanes.*

*Ode 1. Heirmos: Traversing the deep of the Red Sea with dryshod feet...*

*Troparion:*

Let us glorify the God-Originating Hypostasis in Three Persons, co-everlasting, co-enthroned, entreating Whom we say: Save those who praise Thee with faith!

*Troparion:*

The Son was anointed by the Father with the Spirit of joy, with the beneficent oil; and He became man, and taught the three Hypostases of the one Godhead.

*Troparion:*

The Seraphim, not enduring to gaze upon the beauty of Thine unapproachable glory, O Tri-Solar Unity, cover themselves with their wings, and with thrice-holy hymns unceasingly praise Thee.

*Theotokion:*

Ineffably didst thou bear the Creator of all, O Most Pure Lady, Who delivers men from the ancient curse; and saved by thee, through thee we have known the one Tri-hypostatic God.

*Ode 3. Heirmos: Not in wisdom, nor in power, nor in wealth...*

*Troparion:* (fol. 57)[^75]

Having sent down from on high the Comforter from the Father unto Thine holy Apostles of old, O Christ, Thou didst reveal the one Tri-Solar Nature.

*Troparion:*

When Thou didst appear of old unto the Patriarch Abraham in the form of men, O immutable Trinitarian Unity, Thou didst show forth [the mystery] of Thy goodness and Lordship; Thou Who art believed in as the one God in three forms, visibly uncircumscribed, incomprehensible unto all: deliver our souls from every affliction!

*Theotokion:*

Guided by the all-wise teachings of thy Son, we glorify the unique and tri-radiant Godhead, and we bless thee, O Ever-Virgin.

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 4 [after Ode 3]:*

O Tri-Solar, uncreated, and consubstantial Unity, Tri-hypostatic and incomprehensible: take pity upon Thy servants; save us from perils, as Thou art merciful; for we have acquired Thee, O Lord, as Redeemer and Master, crying: Be merciful unto us!

*Glory, both now. Theotokion:*

We hymn thee, O Bride of God, Mother of Christ God, praising [p62LA] thy birthgiving, incomprehensible, whereby we were delivered from the devil's deceit and from all assaults, O Sovereign Lady, Mother of God; therefore with faith we cry unto thee: Have mercy upon thy flock, O only Supremely Hymned One!

*Ode 4. Heirmos: Seated in glory upon the throne of the Godhead...*

*Troparion:*

The super-essential Trinity in the Unity of the Godhead and Lordship, with the Seraphim we glorify Thee as indivisible in Nature, as unapproachable, as co-equal in glory, O incomprehensible God!

*Troparion:*

Incomprehensibly divided in Persons of the Godhead, yet united together in dominion in one Lordship, boundless, one, uncircumscribed, we hymn Thee, the Creator of all creation.

*Troparion:*

The unoriginate Mind ineffably begot the Word, and the Divine Spirit is co-equal in power; and on this account we proclaim the consubstantial Trinity, the God and Master of all things.

*Theotokion:*

Being seen in figures by the ancients, the Word pre-announced His incarnation from thee, O Virgin; yet in these latter times He appeared unto men, truly revealing the Tri-hypostatic Monarchy.

*Ode 5. Heirmos: All things marvelled at thy divine [memory]...*

*Troparion:* (fol. 58)[^76]

Having understood through faith the all-effecting Godhead, we hymn indeed one unapproachable Essence, yet three Hypostases, life-originating and equal: the Father, and the Son, and the Holy Spirit, a co-everlasting existence.

*Troparion:*

O Tri-Solar Light, unique Godhead, uncreated Nature, and light-producing Fountain of every light-giving ray: shine forth upon me Thine essential Light, that I may contemplate Thine ineffable beauty!

*Troparion:*

As unto the only existing Creator of all, the all-wise Sustainer and Helmsman, and truly the Giver of life, faithfully we cry unto Thee: O Tri-Solar Master, preserve those who hymn Thee!

*Theotokion:*

Desiring to give life unto man who had grown old and corrupt of old, whom for the sake of goodness Thy Son created, O Virgin, and showing forth the image of the divine form, He became man from thee, and proclaimed the one Godhead in three Persons.

*Ode 6. Heirmos: Crying aloud, he prefigured the three-day burial...*

[p63LA] *Troparion:*

...The Father appeared, proclaiming the Sonship; and the Spirit was seen as Christ was baptized; on this account we glorify the one and Trinitarian Godhead.

*Troparion:*

When Isaiah beheld Thee seated upon a high throne, praised with thrice-holy voices, he understood the Trinitarian Hypostasis of the one Godhead.

*Troparion:*

Show forth a lifted-up heart also in Thy servants, O exalted King, Tri-hypostatic [God], that we may clearly gaze upon the radiance of Thy glory.

*Theotokion:*

I have sinned, yet I have not departed from Thee, wretched one that I am; have compassion upon me, O Tri-hypostatic, one, honorable Godhead, and deliver me from grievous condemnation through the entreaties of the Maiden of God!

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 4 [after Ode 6]:*

O incomprehensible and unapproachable God, Who art one in Nature, good and merciful: Father, and Son, and Holy Spirit! Preserve and sanctify those who cry out with faith in Thine holy church by night and by day, and deliver us not in any way unto the enemies who seek our destruction!

*Glory, both now. Theotokion:*

We shall never cease, O Ever-Virgin, gratefully to hymn [^77] [f. 59A] from the heart, we thy servants, thy mercies, O Sovereign Lady, calling out and saying: O Most Holy Virgin, go before us, and deliver us from invisible enemies, and from perils, and from every threatening; for thou art our Protection!

*Ode 7. Heirmos: In the fiery furnace the Persian children of...*

*Troparion:*

Imitating the heavenly natures established in rank, and the noetic orders, all we earthborn of the Orthodox glorify one Godhead in three Hypostases equal in action.

*Troparion:*

The eyes of the holy Prophets of old figuratively made Thee known as the only Creator of all the ages, the ineffable God and Lord, in three God-originating Hypostases.

*Theotokion:*

The Word, invisible by essence and all-effecting, Thou didst appear unto men, becoming man from the pure Mother of God, calling man unto communion with Thy Godhead.

*Ode 8. Heirmos: Daniel, stretching forth his hands, [stopped] the gaping mouths of the lions...*

*Troparion:*

O unique and tri-radiant Light, unoriginate Essence, unfathomable Beauty: make Thine abode in my heart, and a radiant temple of Thy Godhead [f. 59A] show me forth radiant and pure, as I cry: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord; sing and exalt Him supremely unto the ages!

*Troparion:*

From my diverse passions, O indivisible Trinity, unconfused Unity, and from the darkening of transgressions deliver me, and illumine me with Thy divine rays, that I may reflect Thy glory and hymn Thee, the Lord of glory!

*Troparion:*

The Father is indeed the unbegotten Mind, and the Word is of like form, and the Spirit is co-enthroned; one Essence, Power, and Existence: O super-essential, ineffable, majestic Trinity and Unity, preserve Thy flock by the intercessions of the Mother of God, as the only Lover of mankind!

*Ode 9. Heirmos: Let all earthborn leap for joy...*

*Troparion:*

Unto Thee now I move all my heart and mind, and the intentions of my whole soul and body, unto my Creator and Redeemer, O tri-radiant Monarchy; and I cry unto Thee: Save me, Thy servant, from perils and all afflictions!

*Troparion:*

Lift up our mind and thought unto Thee on high; illumine us with Thine immaculate splendors, O Father, Word, and Comforter, Who dwellest in light unapproachable [^78] [f. 60A] O Sun of glory and Holder of light, that we may ever glorify Thee, the one Principle, the Tri-hypostatic God!

*Theotokion:*

Save those who believe in Thee, O Lord, and who proclaim the unoriginate, co-everlasting one Essence, and the three God-originating, conformed Persons of Thy Lordship, and make them worthy of divine glory, by the entreaties of the pure Mother of God!

## SUNDAY MATINS (TONE 4)

*On Sunday at Matins, on "God is the Lord," Troparion of the Resurrection. Sessional hymns of the Resurrection, and the canons of the Octoechos to 8, and we also sing other Theotokia.*

*After the First Kathisma, Sessional Hymn of the Resurrection, Tone 4:*

Gazing upon the entrance of the tomb, and not enduring the angelic flame, the Myrrh-bearing women marvelled with trembling, saying: Has He been stolen away, Who opened Paradise unto the thief? Or is He risen, Who even before His Passion proclaimed His arising? Truly Christ is risen, granting life and resurrection unto those in Hades!

*Another Sessional Hymn:*

Thou didst arise as immortal from Hades, O Savior; Thou didst raise up Thy world from bonds; by Thy Resurrection, O Christ our God, Thou didst crush with Thy strength the [f. 60A] dominion; Thou didst show forth the Resurrection unto all, O Merciful One; therefore we glorify Thee, O only Lover of mankind.

*Theotokion:*

We magnify thee, O Mother of God, crying: Thou art the Mountain from which the Stone was ineffably cut without hands, and crushed the gates of Hades.

*After the Second Kathisma, Sessional Hymn of the Resurrection, Tone 4:*

By Thine own free counsel Thou didst endure crucifixion, O Savior, and mortal men laid Thee in a new tomb, Who by a word didst establish the ends [of the earth]; therefore the stranger was bound, death was grievously despoiled, and all those in Hades cried out unto Thy life-bearing arising: Christ the Giver of life is risen, abiding unto the ages!

*Another Sessional Hymn:*

Gabriel descended from the heights of heaven, and approaching the stone where the Rock of life lay, appearing in radiant white, he cried unto the weeping women: Cease from the cry of lamentation, ye who possess ever tender compassion; for Whom ye seek weeping, be of good courage, for He is truly risen; therefore cry out unto the Apostles that the Lord is risen!

*Theotokion:*

Joseph marvelled, beholding that which was beyond nature [^79] [f. 61A] and brought to mind the rain upon the fleece, in thy seedless conception, O Mother of God; the bush unconsumed by fire, Aaron's rod that blossomed; and thy betrothed and guardian, bearing witness, cried unto the priests: A Virgin bears a Child, and after childbirth remains a Virgin still!

*After the Evlogetaria, Hypakoe, Tone 4:*

Going before, the Myrrh-bearing women proclaimed Thy glorious arising unto the Apostles, O Christ: that Thou art risen as God, granting great mercy unto the world!

*Anabathmoi, Antiphon 1, Tone 4:*

From my youth many passions war against me; but do Thou Thyself help and save me, O my Savior.

Ye that hate Zion, be put to shame by the Lord; for ye shall be dried up by fire as grass.

By the Holy Spirit every soul is given life, and is exalted in purity; it is illumined by the Trinitarian Unity in sacred mystery.

*Antiphon 2:*

I have cried unto Thee fervently, O Lord, from the depth of my soul; and let Thine ears be attentive unto me [f. 61A] [even] Thine divine ears.

Everyone who possesses hope in the Lord is higher than all who grieve.

By the Holy Spirit streams of grace gush forth, watering all creation unto life.

*Antiphon 3:*

Let my heart be lifted up unto Thee, O Word, and let nothing of the world's beauties entice me unto weakness.

As anyone has love toward his mother, a warmer love ought we to have toward the Lord.

By the Holy Spirit is the wealth of divine knowledge, contemplation, and wisdom; for in Him the Word reveals all the Father's decrees.

*Prokeimenon, Tone 4:*

Arise, O Lord, help us, and deliver us for Thy name's sake!

*Verse: O God, with our ears have we heard...*

*Let every breath praise the Lord.*

*Glory: Through the prayers of the holy Apostles... Both now: Through the prayers of the Most Pure Lady...*

*Canon of the Resurrection, Tone 4.*

*Ode 1. Heirmos:* (fol. 62)[^80]

Traversing the deep of the Red Sea with dryshod feet, Israel of old, by Moses' hands stretched out in the sign of the Cross, conquered the power of Amalek in the wilderness.

*Troparion:*

Thou wast lifted up upon the immaculate Tree of the Cross, setting upright our fall, and healing ancient total destruction, O Master, for Thou art good and almighty.

*Troparion:*

In the tomb bodily, in Hades with the soul as God, in Paradise with the thief, and on the throne wast Thou, O Christ, with the Father and the Spirit, filling all things, Thyself uncircumscribed.

*Theotokion:*

Seedlessly, by the Father's will and from the Divine Spirit, having conceived the Son of God, thou didst bear Him in the flesh, Who is from the Father without mother, and for our sake from thee without father.

*Second Canon [Cross-Resurrection], Heirmos: I shall open my mouth...*

*Troparion:*

Thou didst heal the crushing of mankind, O Lord; by Thy divine Blood Thou didst renew it, and didst crush him that was mighty in strength, who of old had crushed Thy creation.

*Troparion:*

Arising came unto the dead on account of Thy putting to death; for strength was taken away from death when it contended with eternal Life, which reigns over all in God incarnate...

[f. 61A] *Theotokion:*

...Fair beyond the heavenly hosts was Thine animate divine temple, having borne in the womb, O Virgin, the holy Mountain of our God.

*Another Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Heirmos: The captains of three ranks...*

*Troparion:*

Peoples shook, nations were troubled, and mighty kingdoms bowed down, O Pure One, from the fear of thy childbearing; for my King came, cast down the tormentor, and delivered the world from corruption.

*Troparion:*

Showing forth His dwelling on high, coming down unto men, O Christ, Thou didst sanctify and reveal it unshakable; for she alone after childbirth preserved the virginal vessel, having borne the Creator.

*Ode 3. Heirmos: Thy Church rejoices in Thee, O Christ, crying: Thou art my strength, O Lord, my refuge and my firm support!*

*Troparion:*

The Tree of life, the noetic true Vine, hangs upon the Tree, pouring forth incorruption unto all.

*Troparion:*

How great, how fearful! having overthrown the haughtiness of Hades, and as incorrupt God, He is now bodily risen!

*Theotokion:*

Thou alone for those on earth wast shown to be the Mediatress of supernatural goods, O Mother of God; therefore we bring unto thee "Rejoice!"

*Another Heirmos [Cross-Resurrection]: Establish thy singers, O Mother of God...* (fol. 63)[^81]

*Troparion:*

The serpent full of poison drove his teeth into me, O Savior; but Thou, O Almighty Lord, didst shatter them with the nails of Thy hands; for there is none holy among the saints save Thee, O Lover of mankind!

*Troparion:*

Being willingly seen dead in the tomb, O Lover of mankind and Giver of life, Thou didst fling wide the gates of Hades unto the souls from the ages; for there is none holy among the saints save Thee, O Lover of mankind!

*Theotokion:*

An unplowed furrow wast thou revealed, having brought forth the living Ear of grain that mediates immortality unto all that partake of Him Who is Holy among the saints, resting in holiness.

*Another Heirmos [to the Most Holy Theotokos]: He descended from on high...*

*Troparion:*

The essence of earthborn is cleansed, being joined through thee unto the unendurable Divine Fire; as upon embers, O Most Pure Virgin, the Bread was baked within thee, by Him Who preserved thee unharmed.

*Troparion:*

Who is this that is truly so close unto God, having surpassed all the angelic orders, alone by the beauty of virginity, shining as Mother of the Almighty?

[f. 61A] *Ode 4. Heirmos: Beholding Thee, the Sun of righteousness, lifted up...*

*Troparion:*

Thou didst ascend the Cross, healing my passions by the passion of Thine immaculate flesh, wherewith Thou didst willingly clothe Thyself; therefore we cry unto Thee: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!

*Troparion:*

Death, tasting of Thy sinless, life-creating Body, was deservedly put to death; and we cry unto Thee: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!

*Theotokion:*

Without bridegroom didst thou bear, O Virgin, and after childbearing thou wast shown to be a virgin still; therefore with unceasing voices, with unhesitating faith we cry unto thee, O Sovereign Lady: Rejoice!

*Another Heirmos [Cross-Resurrection]: Perceiving the inscrutable divine counsel...*

*Troparion:*

Israel, which had received the law, knew Thee not to be God Who had given the law, O Christ; but transgressing the law, like a lawless one they nailed Thee to the Cross, they that were unworthy of the giving of the law.

*Troparion:*

Thy deified soul, O Savior, despoiled the treasures of Hades, and raised the souls from the ages; [^82] [f. 64A] and Thy life-bearing Body poured forth incorruption unto all.

*Theotokion:*

Thee, the truly only Ever-Virgin Mother of God, we glorify, whom the unconsumed burning bush prefigured unto Moses the God-beholder, O Most Pure Lady, having joined with fire.

*Another Heirmos [to the Most Holy Theotokos], the same:*

*Troparion:*

He lived among men; He Who is invisible was seen in the form of incomprehensible Godhead; and taking form from thee, O Virgin, He delivers from the stranger those who acknowledge thee as the pure Mother of God.

*Troparion:*

The Virgin received materially the Immaterial One in the midst of matter, Who became an Infant from her; therefore in two natures is one known: God bearing flesh, and earthly, yet above nature.

*Theotokion:*

He Who dwelt in thee, the Virgin, preserved thee a virgin after childbirth; and born seedlessly, the Word and God remained, and in childbearing preserved thee a Virgin, as Master and Creator of all creation.

*Ode 5. Heirmos: Thou, my Lord, art come as a light into the world: a holy light turning from gloomy ignorance those who hymn Thee with faith.*

*Troparion:*

Thou, O Lord, didst mercifully come down unto the earth; Thou didst lift up fallen human nature [f. 64A] being lifted up upon the Tree.

*Troparion:*

Thou didst take away from me the condemnation of transgression, O Christ; Thou didst loose the pangs of death, O Compassionate One, by Thy divine Resurrection!

*Theotokion:*

Thee do we set forth as an invincible weapon against enemies; thee have we acquired as firm support and hope of our salvation, O Bride of God!

*Another Heirmos [Cross-Resurrection]: All things marvelled at thy divine [memory]...*

*Troparion:*

Abyssal Hades received Thee wholly with its jaws; for seeing Thee nailed to the Cross, pierced by a spear, breathless, the simple one supposed the living God to be a mere man; but having tried the strength of Thy Godhead, he understood.

*Troparion:*

The tomb and Hades, having divided the destroyed temple of Thy Body, O Lover of mankind, are both unwillingly judged and despoiled: the one indeed sending forth the souls of Thy saints, and the other their bodies unto immortality.

*Theotokion:*

Behold, now is the prophetic utterance fulfilled; for thou, O unwedded Virgin, didst receive in thy womb God Who is over all, and didst bear the timeless Son, Who grants peace unto all that hymn thee.

*Another Heirmos [to the Most Holy Theotokos]: Now shall I arise, [the Lord said]...*

*Troparion:* (fol. 65)[^83]

The Son of God, having dwelt within thee, O Pure One, made thee a house of glory, the holy Mountain of God, bride, bridal chamber, temple of sanctification, and delightful, everlasting Paradise for us.

*Troparion:*

From the virginal blood Thou didst take flesh, O Christ, seedless, immaculate, and hypostatic, endowed with word and mind, animate, active and endowed with will, sovereign and self-determining.

*Theotokion:*

The virginal womb put to shame the understanding of tyrants; for the Infant examined with His hand the soul-destroying bite of the asp, and cast down the proud apostate, subduing him under the feet of the faithful.

*Ode 6. Heirmos: I will sacrifice unto Thee with the voice of praise, O Lord, the Church cries out unto Thee, having been cleansed from the blood of demons by the Blood that flowed in mercy from Thy side.*

*Troparion:*

Thou didst ascend the Cross, girded with power, and grappling with the tormentor as God, Thou didst cast him down from on high; and Adam Thou didst raise up by Thine invincible power.

*Troparion:*

Radiantly beautiful, Thou didst arise from the tomb, O Christ, and didst scatter all Thine enemies by Thy divine [f. 64A] power; and as God Thou didst fill all things with joy.

*Theotokion:*

O wonder, of all wonders the newest! For the Virgin, having conceived without knowing man, unconfinedly contained in her womb Him Who holds all things.

*Another Heirmos [Cross-Resurrection]: I came into the depths [of the sea]...*

*Troparion:*

Hades opened its throat and swallowed me, and the senseless one expanded its soul; but Christ having descended, bring up my life, O Lover of mankind!

*Troparion:*

Death perished by death; for He that had died arose, granting incorruption unto me; and the Immortal One appeared unto us, proclaiming joy.

*Theotokion:*

Thy pure womb was revealed as the receptacle of the unendurable Godhead, O Mother of God, upon Whom the heavenly ranks cannot gaze without fear.

*Another Heirmos [to the Most Holy Theotokos], the same:*

*Troparion:*

Of old the serpent deceived me and put me to death through my foremother Eve; but now, O Pure One, He Who created me called me back from corruption through thee.

*Theotokion:*

An abyss of abysses, of ineffable tender compassion, did He show thee forth, O chosen Maiden of wonders; for from thee Christ, the Pearl, shone forth with the lightning of the Godhead.

*Kontakion, Tone 4.*

*Podoben: "Thou hast appeared today" (Явился еси):* [^84]

My Savior and Redeemer, as God, having raised the earthborn from bonds from the tomb, and crushed the gates of Hades, arose on the third day as Master!

*Ikos:*

The Giver of life, Christ, Who arose from the dead on the third day from the tomb, and today crushed the gates of death by His power, and put Hades to death, and blunted the sting of death, and set Adam free with Eve: let all us earthborn hymn, eagerly crying out praise! For He, as the only mighty God and Master, arose on the third day!

*Ode 7. Heirmos: In the furnace the Abrahamite children, burned by the desire of piety rather than by the Persian flame, cried out: Blessed art Thou in the temple of Thy glory, O Lord!*

*Troparion:*

Unto incorruption was mankind recalled, washed by the divine Blood of Christ, gratefully singing: Blessed art Thou in the temple of Thy glory, O Lord!

*Troparion:*

As life-bearing, as truly fairer than Paradise, and more radiant than any royal chamber, O Christ, was Thy tomb revealed: the fountain of our resurrection! [f. 64A] divine and holy dwelling of the Most High! Rejoice, for through thee joy is given, O Mother of God, unto those who cry unto thee: Blessed art thou among women, all-immaculate Lady!

*Another Heirmos [Cross-Resurrection]: He Who bears all things...*

*Troparion:*

Thou didst humble, being lifted up upon the Tree, the lofty eye and the exalted brow, casting them down unto the earth, and Thou didst save man! O supremely hymned Lord and God of our fathers, blessed art Thou!

*Troparion:*

By Thy power, Thou didst exalt our horn, unto us who give ear unto Thee, Who didst arise from the dead and in Thy great love for mankind didst empty the wealth of Hades! O supremely hymned Lord and God of our fathers, blessed art Thou!

*Troparion:*

Following the divine decrees, we glorify the one Godhead as in three Lights, unconfused, indivisible, an everlasting flame enlightening all creation that cries: O supremely hymned Lord and God of our fathers, blessed art Thou!

*Another Heirmos [to the Most Holy Theotokos]: The three youths in Babylon...*

*Troparion:*

Virginal love draws me unto singing, the fire which is in my heart, to cry unto the Mother: Rejoice, blessed one, the Lord of hosts is with thee!

*Troparion:*

Thou wast revealed higher than creation, having borne the Creator (fol. 67)[^85] and Lord; therefore I cry unto thee: O blessed Mother of God, the Lord of hosts is with thee!

*Triadikon:*

Honoring Thee as one Lordship in three illuminations, indivisibly I hymn the Tri-hypostatic Nature: O Blessed One, to praise Thee Who governest all things!

*Ode 8. Heirmos: Daniel, stretching out his hands, stopped the open mouths of the lions in the pit; and the Children, lovers of piety, girded about with virtue, quenched the power of the fire, crying: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord; sing and supremely exalt Him unto the ages!*

*Troparion:*

Stretching out Thy hands upon the Cross, Thou didst gather all the nations unto Thyself, and as Master Thou didst reveal one Church, earthly and heavenly, singing unto Thee, in harmony crying: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord; sing and supremely exalt Him unto the ages!

*Troparion:*

White-robed appeared the Angel unto the women, flashing with the inaccessible light of the Resurrection: Why do ye seek the Living One in the tomb as dead? he cried; truly Christ is risen, unto Whom we cry: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord; sing and supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Theotokion:*

Thou alone in all generations, O Most Pure Virgin, wast shown to be the Mother [f. 64A] of God; thou becamest the dwelling of the Godhead, all-immaculate one, unconsumed by the fire of the unapproachable Light; therefore all of us bless thee, O Mary, Bride of God!

*Another Heirmos [Cross-Resurrection]: The godly youths in the furnace...*

*Troparion:*

Beholding Thine unjust slaying, creation darkened and lamented; for as the earth was shaken, the sun was clothed in darkness as in a crimson robe; but we sing of Thee, O Christ, and supremely exalt Thee unto all ages!

*Troparion:*

Descending unto me even unto Hades, and making the Resurrection a way for all, Thou didst ascend again, taking me upon Thy shoulders, and didst bring me unto the Father; therefore I cry unto Thee: Works, sing the Lord, and supremely exalt Him unto the ages!

*Triadikon:*

We glorify the first Mind and Cause of all, the Father alone, Who has no cause; and the Word Who is co-unoriginate, and the Comforter Spirit, the one God of all, bowing down before the equally unoriginate Trinity, and supremely exalting It unto all ages!

*Another Heirmos [to the Most Holy Theotokos]: Almighty Redeemer of all...*

*Troparion:*

He Who fashioned the frame of Adam is incarnate from thy virginity, the Lord of all, Whom hymning (fol. 68)[^86] we cry: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord; sing and supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Troparion:*

In the tent Abraham beheld the mystery concerning thee, O Mother of God; for he received thy bodiless Son, singing: All works, bless the Lord; sing and supremely exalt Him unto the ages!

*Triadikon:*

The prefiguration of thy virginity saved those equal in number to the Trinity; for in virginal bodies they trampled the flame, O Maiden, crying: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord; sing and supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Ode 9. Heirmos: A cornerstone uncut by hands was cut out from thee, an uncut mountain, O Virgin, even Christ, Who joined together the separated natures; therefore rejoicing, we magnify thee, O Mother of God!*

*Troparion:*

Whole as I was, Thou didst wholly assume me in unconfused union, granting salvation unto my whole being, O my God, through Thy Passion, which Thou didst endure bodily upon the Cross, by reason of Thy manifold tender compassion!

*Troparion:*

Thy Disciples, beholding Thy tomb opened and the God-bearing Shroud [Plashchanytsia] emptied by Thy Resurrection, with the Angel cried aloud: Truly the Lord is risen [f. 64A] *Triadikon:*

...Bowing down before the unity of the divine essence, yet Three Persons of the Trinity, in unconfused Hypostases, all we faithful now piously magnifying glorify the equal in power and equally honored!

*Another Heirmos [Cross-Resurrection]: The Savior, born of earth...*

*Troparion:*

Deceitfully creeping, the serpent dragged me captive from Eden; but the Almighty Lord, crushing the adversary upon the firm Rock of Cranium [Calvary] as if an infant, through the Tree of the Cross opened unto me an entrance into the sweetness [of Paradise]!

*Troparion:*

Having now laid waste the strong fortresses of the enemy, and plundered his wealth by Thine all-powerful hand, Thou didst raise me up with Thyself, O Christ, from the depths of Hades; and him that of old boasted immeasurably, Thou didst show to be an object of derision and mockery!

*Troparion:*

Come, visiting the affliction of Thy poor people, and by Thy merciful and mighty right hand strengthen the Cross-bearing King against the boastful enemies, and protect Thy choice heritage, as Thou art the Lover of mankind!

*Another Heirmos [to the Most Holy Theotokos]: The mystery of God, O Most Pure One...*

*Troparion:* (fol. 69)[^87]

We behold thee, O Most Pure One, as a lily adorned with a vesture dyed in the purple of the Divine Spirit, shining in the midst of thorns and filling with fragrance those who sincerely magnify thee!

*Troparion:*

Having received corruptible earthly nature from thine all-immaculate womb, the Incorruptible One showed it forth incorruptible within Himself, by reason of His tender compassion; therefore we magnify thee as the Mother of God!

*Troparion:*

O thou that rulest over all creation, grant unto thy people victorious triumph, overthrowing the adversary, O Reconciler of the Church, that we may magnify thee as the Mother of God!

*At the Praises, Stichera of the Resurrection, Tone 4:*

*Sticheron 1:*

O almighty Lord, Who didst endure crucifixion and death, and didst arise from the dead: we glorify Thy Resurrection!

*Sticheron 2:*

By Thy Cross, O Christ, Thou didst deliver us from the ancient curse; and by Thy death Thou didst bring to naught the devil who tormented our nature; and by Thine arising Thou didst fill all things with joy; therefore we cry unto Thee: O Thou Who didst arise from the dead, Lord, glory to Thee!

[f. 64A] *Sticheron 3:*

By Thy Cross, O Christ our Savior, guide us unto Thy truth, and deliver us from the snares of the enemy, O Thou Who didst arise from the dead: raise us up who have fallen into sin, stretching out Thy hand, O Lord, Lover of mankind, by the entreaties of Thy saints!

*Sticheron 4:*

Without departing from the Father's bosom, O only-begotten Word of God, Thou didst come down unto the earth for the sake of Thy love for mankind, having become man without change; and Thou didst endure crucifixion and death in the flesh, while remaining passionless in Thy Godhead; and having arisen from the dead, Thou didst grant immortality unto the human race, as Thou alone art almighty!

*Other Stichera, Anatolian [Eastern], the same Tone:*

*Sticheron 5:*

Thou didst submit unto death in the flesh, mediating immortality for us, O Savior; and Thou didst take up Thine abode in the tomb, that Thou mightest free us from Hades, raising us up with Thyself; Thou didst suffer indeed as man, but Thou didst arise as God; therefore we cry: Glory to Thee, O Lord, Giver of life, only Lover of mankind!

*Sticheron 6:*

The rocks split asunder, O Savior, when Thy Cross was planted upon Calvary; the gatekeepers of Hades were terrified when Thou wast laid in the tomb as dead (fol. 70)[^88]; for Thou didst bring to naught the power of death, and Thou didst grant incorruption unto all the dead by Thy Resurrection, O Christ, Giver of life, Lord, glory to Thee!

*Verse: Arise, O Lord my God, let Thy hand be lifted up; forget not Thy poor unto the end.*

*Sticheron 7:*

The women desired to see Thy Resurrection, O Christ God; Mary Magdalene went beforehand and came, and found the stone rolled away from the tomb, and an Angel sitting and saying: Why do ye seek the Living One with the dead? He is risen as God, that He might save all things!

*Verse: I will give thanks unto Thee, O Lord, with my whole heart; I will speak of all Thy marvelous works.*

*Sticheron 8:*
