Thy martyrs, O Lord, forgot the things of this life, disregarding the fleeting age for the sake of the life to come: they were revealed as heirs thereof; therefore they rejoice with the Angels: by their prayers grant Thy people great mercy!

*Sticheron 2:*

If there be any virtue, and if there be any praise, it is meet for the saints: for by the sword they bowed their necks, for the sake of Thee Who didst bow...

(see fol. 195)[^214]

...the heavens and come down; they shed their blood for the sake of Thee Who didst empty Thyself and take the form of a servant; they humbled themselves even unto death, conforming themselves unto Thy poverty: through the multitude of Thy compassions, O God, have mercy on us!

*Hymn for the Departed (Поко́инъ):*

I weep and lament when I contemplate death, and behold our beauty, created after the image of God, lying in the tombs: formless, inglorious, having neither form nor comeliness. O wonder! What is this mystery that has befallen us? How have we been given over unto corruption? And how have we been yoked unto death? In truth, by the command of God, as it is written, Who grants rest unto the departed.

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*

O Sovereign Lady, accept the supplications of thy servants, and deliver us from every distress and affliction.

## SATURDAY MATINS: ALL SAINTS, MARTYRS, AND THE DEPARTED (TONE 8)

*On Saturday at Matins, after the first Kathisma, Sessional Hymns to the Martyrs, Tone 8:*

*Sessional Hymn 1:*

With celestial light creation is enlightened today, even this temple; for in it the angelic hosts rejoice, and with them the choirs of martyrs make glad, in the memory of the passion-bearers: by their prayers send down, O Christ, unto our souls peace and great mercy.

*Glory... Sessional Hymn 2:*

As noetic luminaries were ye revealed, O holy martyrs, unto the world [f. 136A] for ye abolished deceit by faith, and enlightened your spiritual lamps, and entered with the Bridegroom in glory into the heavenly bridal chamber; and now pray that our souls may be saved.

*Both now... Theotokion: "Who for our sake wast born of the Virgin..."*

*After the second Kathisma, Sessional Hymns to the Martyrs, Tone 8.*

*Podoben: "Guiding unto wisdom" (Премꙋ́дрости наста́вниче):*

*Sessional Hymn 1:*

As ever-shining luminaries unto the universe were ye revealed through faith, having in martyr-like manner placed all hope upon the Lord, and with the noetic oil of the Holy Spirit ye enlightened your spiritual lamps; therefore also as spiritual chalices, pouring forth your blood like water, ye appeared unto the Church, O all-lauded passion-bearers: pray Christ God to grant the remission of sins unto us who with love honor your holy memory.

*Sessional Hymn 2:*

Valiant martyrs of the Lord, and hierarchs: who having lawfully and honorably accomplished the good fight, and received incorruptible crowns from God, unceasingly pray His goodness for us, that He may grant us remission of transgressions, as He is a God easily reconciled.

*Glory... Sessional Hymn for the Departed:*

With depth of wisdom, in love for mankind, Thou orderest all things, and dispensest unto all that which is profitable, O only Creator: give rest, O Lord, unto the souls of Thy servants; for they have placed their hope in Thee, our Maker and Creator and God.

*Both now... Theotokion:*

In thee do we have a wall and a haven, and an acceptable...

(see fol. 196)[^215]

...intercessor before the God Who was born of thee, O unwedded Mother of God: thou art the salvation of the faithful.

*Canon to the Martyrs, Hierarchs, Venerable Ones, and the Departed, Tone 8.*

*Ode 1. Heirmos:*

Let us sing unto the Lord, Who led His people through the Red Sea: a song of victory, for He is glorified.

*Holy Martyrs, pray to God for us.*

*Troparion 1:*

Ye were revealed as precious pearls, and ye adorned the crown of the honorable Church, O valiant passion-bearers of Christ.

*Troparion 2:*

The hierarchs of Christ shone with the divine brightness of dogmas and virtues, and thereby enlightened the hearts of the faithful.

*Troparion 3:*

We entreat Thee, O Word, Who art marvelous in the Prophets and in the Venerable: by their prayers save us.

*Hymn for the Departed (Поко́инъ):*

When Thou, the most righteous Judge, comest to execute righteous judgment, O Word: save us uncondemned by their prayers.

*Theotokion:*

Knowing thee to have born God, O Virgin, the choir of women, having suffered, was brought after thee unto Him.

*Another Canon for the Departed, Tone 8.*

*Ode 1. Heirmos: Traversing the water...*

*Verse: God is marvelous in His saints, the God of Israel.*

*Troparion 1:*

Emulating by their death the death of Christ, and by their sufferings His honorable Passion, all the martyrs obtained the divine and blessed life.

[f. 136A] *Troparion 2:*

Overlooking the transgressions of youth and passing by the sins, O Christ Savior: number among Thine elect Thy servants who have fallen asleep before us,

*Troparion 3:*

Granting richly unto Thy servants whom Thou hast received, O much-merciful One, the glory and the joy which they that acquired the blessed abode have obtained.

*Theotokion:*

Thou didst conceive the Word of the Father, hypostatically united in the flesh, Who through thee, O all-immaculate Maiden, abolished hades by divine power.

*Canon to the Martyrs, Hierarchs, and Venerable.*

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*

O Word Who by the word didst establish the heavens: establish our mind and heart, that we may glorify Thee, the Savior of our souls.

*Troparion 1:*

Having rejected the abominable sacrifices with steadfast mind, the sufferers became a most honorable sacrifice unto the Word Who was sacrificed.

*Troparion 2:*

Renewing by hierurgic words that which had grown old through all kinds of passions, ye were revealed as divine disciples of Him Who renewed the world.

*Troparion 3:*

The grace of the All-Holy Spirit, which of old was manifestly given unto the Prophets, afterwards filled the fasting ascetics with divine gifts.

*Hymn for the Departed (Поко́инъ):*

Number among the choirs of the saints, O God, those who have departed from this life in faith, and make them dwellers of Paradise, for the sake of Thine ineffable mercy.

*Theotokion:*

Jesus, Who is...

(see fol. 197)[^216]

...nowhere contained, roomily took up His dwelling within thy sanctified womb, O all-pure, all-hymned Virgin.

*Another Canon for the Departed.*

*Ode 3. Heirmos: O Lord, Builder of the vault of heaven...*

*Troparion 1:*

Having been cleansed from the ancient ancestral fall by Baptism and regeneration, and sprinkled with the streams of your own blood, O blessed ones, ye reign with Christ.

*Troparion 2:*

O Thou Who willingly wast laid dead in the tomb, raising up those who dwell in the tombs, O Savior: be well-pleased that they whom Thou hast taken from among us may dwell in the tents of Thy righteous.

*Troparion 3:*

Being entreated by the divine compassion, and by the grace of Thy nature which is known to be so great: grant forgiveness of sins unto Thy servants, and give them rest with Thyself.

*Theotokion:*

He Who was incarnate of thy womb and became man, Who is pre-eminently the only Lover of mankind, saves man from the gates of death, O all-pure Mother of God, only all-hymned one.

*Kontakion, Tone 8.*

*Podoben: "As first-fruits of nature" (Я҆́кѡ нача́тки е҆стества̀):*

As the first-fruits of nature, unto the Creator of creation the universe offers Thee, O Lord, the God-bearing martyrs: by their prayers preserve in profound peace Thy Church and Thy city, for the sake of the Mother of God, O Thou Who alone art much-merciful.

*Canon to the Martyrs, Hierarchs, and Venerable.*

*Ode 4. Heirmos: I have heard, O Lord...*

*Troparion 1:*

Having entered the spiritual arena, burning in spirit, O divinely-blessed sufferers: ye burned up material deceit.

[f. 136A] *Troparion 2:*

Thy venerable ones, O Lord, and the hierarchs hast Thou radiant-glorified: by their divine prayers make us partakers of Thy glory.

*Troparion 3:*

The inspiration of the Divine Spirit which enlightened the Prophets granted strength unto women, and they destroyed the haughty insolence of the enemy.

*Hymn for the Departed (Поко́инъ):*

Deign to grant that Thy servants whom Thou hast translated, O Most Good One, may rejoice in the choir together with all the saints in the dwellings of Thy saints, we entreat Thee.

*Theotokion:*

Above the laws of nature, O unwedded Bride of God, thou didst bear the Law-giver of the Divine Father, Who renews fallen human nature.

*Another Canon for the Departed.*

*Ode 4. Heirmos: Thou art my Lord, O Christ, Thou art...*

*Troparion 1:*

That they might behold Thy glory, O Master, and radiantly obtain the light-giving beams of Thy brightness in heaven, the divine martyrs endured to bear every manner of torment, singing unto Thee, O Christ: Glory to Thy power, O Lover of mankind!

*Troparion 2:*

Many are the mansions with Thee, O Savior, apportioned according to worth unto all, according to the measure of virtues: deign, therefore, O Bountiful One, to fill them with those who have departed in faith, piously crying unto Thee: Glory to Thy power, O Lover of mankind!

*Troparion 3:*

Manifestly didst Thou appear unto man, O Immortal One, Thou didst endure death common to all, and didst show forth the paths of life: deign, as Lover of mankind, to grant forgiveness of sins unto those who have departed, as Master bestowing and granting communion of light.

*Theotokion:*

Thou art the boast of the faithful...

(see fol. 198)[^217]

...O unwedded one, thou art the mediatress, thou art the refuge of Christians, a wall and a haven; unto thy Son dost thou bear supplications, O all-immaculate one, and dost save from calamities those who know thee in faith and love as the pure Mother of God.

*Canon to the Martyrs, Hierarchs, and Venerable.*

*Ode 5. Heirmos: Enlighten us by Thy commandments...*

*Troparion 1:*

The divine martyrs endured bodily wounds, and therefore ever heal our wounds, wounding the demons.

*Troparion 2:*

O holy hierarchs of God, pray with all the venerable ones unto Christ, that He may grant us remission of sins.

*Troparion 3:*

Having mortified the flesh by wounds and asceticism, the venerable women, O Lover of mankind, were accounted worthy of the good things hoped for.

*Hymn for the Departed (Поко́инъ):*

Having slain death by Thy death, O Immortal One: as Lover of mankind give rest unto Thy faithful servants who have died in hope of life.

*Theotokion:*

Thou didst abolish the condemnation of our forebears, O pure one, having borne in the flesh Jesus, the only Lord Who justifies all.

*Another Canon for the Departed.*

*Ode 5. Heirmos: Why hast Thou cast me off from Thy...*

*Troparion 1:*

Having compassion, deign to account the departed worthy of Thine ineffable glory and of Thy blessedness which is beyond word, in the dwellings of the saints, where is the delightful voice of those that keep festival in the passionless life, O only Lover of mankind.

*Troparion 2:*

Where are the angelic ranks, where the assemblies of the righteous rejoice [f. 136A] settle Thy servants in the bosom of Abraham, O Savior, and be well-pleased, O Merciful One, that they may stand with boldness before the dread and divine Throne.

*Troparion 3:*

Thou wast revealed as our cleansing, our righteousness, and redemption, and by Thy wound Thou hast healed our infirmities: do Thou therefore, O Bountiful and Good One, appoint those who have departed to dwell in the sweetness of Paradise.

*Theotokion:*

Condescending in mercy, O Compassionate One, Thou didst take upon Thyself the measure of human nature, adorned with divine glory in the highest, taking from virginal loins flesh ensouled and rational, whereby Thou didst destroy death.

*Canon to the Martyrs, Hierarchs, and Venerable.*

*Ode 6. Heirmos: Cleanse me, O Savior, for many are mine...*

*Troparion 1:*

By your honorable wounds ye heal spiritual infirmities, and ever remove the festerings of bodies, wounding the hosts of wicked demons, O holy martyrs.

*Troparion 2:*

The choir of the venerable, the assembly of hierarchs, and the divine company of holy women, having suffered steadfastly, inherited the heavenly rewards.

*Troparion 3:*

Having mortified the flesh, the ascetics received life; and having shepherded well the flock of Christ, the most wise hierarchs were accounted worthy of immortal glory after their end.

*Hymn for the Departed (Поко́инъ):*

Thou Who art life unto the living, O Word, and rest unto the dead...

(see fol. 199)[^218]

...settle Thy departed servants, who have been taken from among us by Thy divine nod, in the bosom of Abraham Thy pleasing servant.

*Theotokion:*

The radiance of the Father was made manifest in thee, O pure one; and having dispelled the darkness of polytheism by the immaterial beams of His Godhead, He enlightened the world.

*Another Canon for the Departed.*

*Ode 6. Heirmos: Cleanse me, O Savior, for many are mine...*

*Troparion 1:*

Thy martyrs, O Savior, endured many tortures, their souls having been wounded by Thy love, O Holy One, desiring Thine everlasting glory and sweet communion with Thee.

*Troparion 2:*

Thou didst tear apart the bonds of inimical death, O Savior, and didst resurrect all the prisoners therein, granting life: whereunto, O Benefactor, deign to admit the departed.

*Troparion 3:*

From tears and sighings in hades deliver Thy servants, O Savior; for as the only Compassionate One, Thou wipest away every tear from every face of those who faithfully theologize Thee.

*Theotokion:*

The Creator of nature is fashioned within thy womb; being full as God, He empties Himself, O all-immaculate one; the only Immortal One dies by death for the salvation of all.

*Kontakion, Tone 8:*

With the saints give rest, O Christ, unto the souls of Thy servants, where there is neither sickness, nor sorrow, nor sighing, but life everlasting.

*Ikos:*

Thou alone art immortal, Who hast made and fashioned man [f. 136A] for we mortals were fashioned from the earth, and unto the same earth shall we go, as Thou didst command Who didst fashion me and say unto me: For dust thou art, and unto dust shalt thou return; whither all we mortals go, making our funeral weeping the song: Alleluia.

*Canon to the Martyrs, Hierarchs, and Venerable.*

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*

The godly children in Babylon bowed not down unto the golden image, but bedewed in the midst of the fiery furnace, sang a hymn, saying: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Troparion 1:*

Having broken down the wall of impiety by your holy bonds, O all-lauded ones: disperse the burden of my sins, as I cry: Martyrs, save me who sing: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Troparion 2:*

With the showers of your holy blood ye quench the fire of heresies; and by flame, O most wise hierarchs, ye burn up the deceitful tares of impiety, and enlighten the souls of the faithful.

*Troparion 3:*

Having mortified the flesh through abstinence, the ascetics live after death; and the choir of the prophets and righteous, and the company of women, having suffered, were glorified: by their prayers, O Christ, deliver us from calamities.

*Hymn for the Departed (Поко́инъ):*

Give rest, O Christ, unto the souls of all who have fallen asleep in hope of life, overlooking their sins committed in this life, according to the multitude of Thy tender mercies, O Compassionate and only Savior, Who art the blessed God of our fathers.

*Theotokion:*

Having received into thy womb...

(see fol. 200)[^219]

...the Word entirely incarnate, as thou art pure: entreat Him, O all-pure one, to cleanse of sins the soul and body of him that flees unto thee and honors thee in faith.

*Another Canon for the Departed.*

*Ode 7. Heirmos: The fire was ashamed before the condescension of...*

*Troparion 1:*

All the desire of the martyrs was extended toward the one Master, being united unto Him in love, and singing: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God of our fathers!

*Troparion 2:*

Thou grantest unto those who have departed in faith the brightness of the Divine Kingdom, bestowing garments of incorruption, as they cry: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God of our fathers!

*Troparion 3:*

Fill with joy and gladness Thy servants whom Thou hast translated, O Bountiful One, having accounted them worthy to call upon Thee and sing unto Thee: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God of our fathers!

*Theotokion:*

Thou didst loose the curse of Eve, for thou didst pour thyself forth as an all-immaculate virgin, pouring forth a wellspring of blessing unto those who cry: Blessed, O all-pure one, is the fruit of thy womb!

*Canon to the Martyrs, Hierarchs, and Venerable.*

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*

The God-proclaiming children in the furnace trampled upon the fire and flame, rejoicing and singing: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Troparion 1:*

Ye cast down the idol temples while suffering, and fashioned yourselves into divine temples of the Trinity, O passion-bearers of the Lord, companions of the Angels.

[f. 136A] *Troparion 2:*

Thy priests, O Christ, the righteous clothed with righteousness, who are from the ages, now rejoice together, beholding most clearly Thy divine beauty.

*Troparion 3:*

By the holy prayers of Thy Prophets, and of the ever-blessed women, and of the righteous from the ages: grant Thy mercies, O Word, unto Thy world.

*Hymn for the Departed (Поко́инъ):*

O Righteous Judge, when Thou shalt come to judge: preserve uncondemned those whom Thou hast taken from among us, overlooking their transgressions, O Master.

*Theotokion:*

Appear, O Maiden, and by thine illumination drive away the dark clouds of my soul, who didst bring forth the Sun of righteousness.

*Another Canon for the Departed.*

*Ode 8. Heirmos: When the musical instruments sounded together...*

*Troparion 1:*

Having passed beyond earthly passions, the true martyrs received heavenly crowns, crying unto Thee unceasingly: Praise ye the Lord, and supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Troparion 2:*

Having descended into the nethermost pit, Thou didst resurrect those that dwell in the tombs by Thy life-originating right hand; and unto Thy servants who have fallen asleep in faith, O Bountiful One, grant rest.

*Troparion 3:*

As Thou art the wellspring of everlasting life, as Thou art the torrent of sweetness: deign that Thy servants who have piously departed unto Thee may sing unto Thee and glorify Thee unto all ages.

*Theotokion:*

O Mary...

(see fol. 201)[^220]

...Virgin Mother of God, who in the flesh didst bear God the Savior unto men: save those who sing of thy childbearing in faith and supremely exalt it unto all ages.

*Canon to the Martyrs, Hierarchs, and Venerable.*

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*

Thee, the unwedded Mother of God and Virgin, who above understanding didst bear by the word the true God, higher than the most pure powers: with unceasing glorifications do we magnify.

*Troparion 1:*

The martyrs stood before the unrighteous tribunal, by the grace of Christ condemning all unrighteousness, rebuking those held by it, and receiving crowns of righteousness.

*Troparion 2:*

Ye appeared as church rudders, piously steering her ship into the haven of God's commandments, all ye blessed shepherd-hierarchs; therefore as helmsmen do we honor you.

*Troparion 3:*

The assembly of the Prophets and the Venerable entreats Thee; the company of women, O Lord, who suffered most radiantly and shone forth in asceticism, entreats Thee, O Lover of mankind: grant us Thy compassions.

*Hymn for the Departed (Поко́инъ):*

Of the honor and eternal glory whereof all the saints of Christ were accounted worthy: deign, O Merciful One, that those who have departed from this vain life in faith may partake, by the prayers of Thy saints.

[f. 136A] *Theotokion:*

Holding the fullness of good things in thy hands, O all-immaculate one: fulfill our supplications, the prayers of thy servants; and direct our journeyings toward God, strengthening us to live in virtues.

*Another Canon for the Departed.*

*Ode 9. Heirmos: Every ear trembled at the ineffable...*

*Troparion 1:*

Possessing the invincible and unconquerable strength of Christ, O martyrs: ye brought to naught the godless decree of the torturers, and manifestly were accounted worthy of the Heavenly Kingdom, enlightened by the beams of the Trinity, O praiseworthy ones.

*Troparion 2:*

Bitter hades was destroyed when Thou didst break it down, O Lover of mankind, resurrecting those who from the ages slept therein; and now, as Thou art good, in peace deign to grant Thine unwaning light unto those who have departed unto Thee.

*Troparion 3:*

Thou art all sweetness, O Savior, Thou art all desire and truly insatiable love, Thou art all ineffable comeliness: be well-pleased, therefore, that those who have departed unto Thee may enjoy Thy beauty, and make them worthy of Thy divine goodness.

*Theotokion:*

Save us, O Mother of God, who didst bear Christ my Savior, both God and man: two-fold in nature, but not in hypostasis, only-begotten of the Father, and from thee the first-born of all creation; Whom in two natures we magnify.

(see fol. 202)[^221]

*Exapostilarion to All Saints:*

O most glorious Prophets, blessed Apostles, righteous Patriarchs, unique assembly of Hierarchs, of the Venerable and Righteous, and company of women, all holy martyrs: together with the Mother of God pray the Lord to have mercy on your servants.

*Glory... For the Departed:*

O Thou Who rulest over the dead and the living as God: give rest unto Thy servants in the dwellings of Thine elect; for even though they sinned, O Savior, yet they departed not from Thee.

*Both now... Theotokion:*

O sweetness of Angels and joy of the sorrowing, mediatress of Christians, Virgin Mother of the Lord: protect us and deliver us from eternal torments.

*At the Praises, Stichera to the Martyrs, Tone 8.*

*Podoben: "What shall we call you, O holy ones" (Что вы наречемъ):*

*Sticheron 1:*

What shall we call you, O holy ones? Cherubim: for upon you Christ has rested; Seraphim: for unceasingly ye glorified Him; Angels: for ye turned away from bodies; Powers: ye work in miracles; many are your names and greater still your gifts: pray that our souls may be saved!

*Sticheron 2:*

Greatly did ye contend, O holy ones: having valiantly endured the torments of the lawless, confessing Christ before kings; and having again departed from this life, ye work powers in the world, and heal the sick by your sufferings, O holy ones: pray that our souls may be saved!

[f. 136A] *Sticheron 3:*

O unconquerable martyrs of Christ: having conquered deceit by the power of the Cross, receiving the grace of eternal life: ye feared not the torturers' threatenings; wounded by torments, ye make glad; and now your blood has become healing for our souls: pray that our souls may be saved!

*Sticheron 4:*

Having well clothed yourselves in the breastplate of faith, and armed yourselves with the weapon of the Cross, ye appeared as strong warriors; ye manfully resisted the torturers and cast down the devil's deceit; having become victors, ye were accounted worthy of crowns: pray that our souls may be saved!

*Sticheron 5, for the Departed:*

Thy death, O Lord, was the mediator of immortality; for hadst Thou not been laid in the tomb, Paradise would not have been opened; therefore give rest unto the departed, as Lover of mankind.

*Glory... Tone 8:*

Countless is the torment for those who live prodigally: the gnashing of teeth, and comfortless weeping, the fiery Gehenna, and the outer darkness, and the sleepless worm, hopeless tears, and the unmerciful Judge: therefore before the end we cry aloud, saying: O Master Christ, give rest unto those whom Thou hast chosen with Thine elect!

*Both now... Theotokion:*

O pure Virgin, gate of the Word, Mother of our God: pray that we may be saved!

(see fol. 203)[^222]

*At the Aposticha, Stichera for the Departed, Tone 8.*

*Podoben: "O most marvelous wonder" (О преслав):*

*Sticheron 1:*

Royally didst Thou sign my freedom, O Master, having reddened Thy fingers with crimson wounds and stained them with Thy Blood; therefore with faith I pray Thee: number with Thy first-born and with Thy righteous, to obtain joy, those who have passed over unto Thee, the Compassionate One.

*Verse: Blessed are they whom Thou hast chosen and taken to Thyself, O Lord.*

*Sticheron 2:*

Thou didst minister as man, having slain Thyself as a Lamb; Thou didst offer an oblation unto the Father, delivering man from corruption: appoint therefore, as Lover of mankind, those who have departed to dwell in the land of the living, where streams of sweetness pour forth, and where ever-flowing fountains gush out.

*Verse: Their souls shall dwell among good things.*

*Sticheron 3:*

By the depths of Thine ineffable wisdom Thou settest a boundary unto life, and foreseest the future, and translatest Thy servants unto another life: those whom Thou hast received, therefore, appoint, O Lord, beside the waters of rest, in the brightness of the saints, and where the voice of praise and rejoicing is heard.

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*

Having conceived the unoriginate Word of God and the Father: with thy maternal boldness entreat Him earnestly to appoint thy servants where is the never-dispersing choir [f. 136A] of those that keep festival and praise thee, where are the everlasting brightnesses, and the sweet voice of those that celebrate, O Mother of God.

*It is good to give praise... Trisagion. Our Father. Troparia.*

*At the Divine Liturgy, the Beatitudes, Tone 8:*

*Troparion 1:*

Remember us, O Christ, Savior of the world, even as Thou rememberedst the Thief upon the Tree, and account all worthy, O only Bountiful One, of Thy Heavenly Kingdom.

*Troparion 2:*

Firmly partaking of all afflictions, by your holy wounds, O passion-bearers, ye wounded all the hosts of the demons by divine grace.

*Troparion 3:*

The venerable ones of Christ, and the divine hierarchs, the assembly of the Prophets, and of all the holy righteous, have obtained the heavenly inheritance; let us worthily call them blessed.

*Troparion 4:*

Appoint all who in faith have departed from this life, O God, in the land of the righteous, and show them forth as heirs of Paradise, who sing of Thee in godlike manner.

*Glory... Trinity Hymn:*

Unto Thee, O Trinity, I offer a final hymn: those whom Thou hast translated hence in faith, make worthy of the dwellings of the saints, and have mercy on me, the prodigal.

*Both now... Theotokion:*

Having borne the Fullness of all good things, fulfill our supplications, O Virgin, asking for us remission of sins, and...

(see fol. 204)[^223]

...enlightenment, and great mercy.

*Prokeimenon, Tone 8: Be glad in the Lord, and rejoice, ye righteous. (Ps. 31:11a) [Verse: And shout for joy, all ye that are upright in heart. (Ps. 31:11b)] And for the Departed, Tone 6: Their souls shall dwell among good things. (Ps. 24:13a) [Verse: Unto Thee, O Lord, have I lifted up my soul. (Ps. 24:1)]*

*The Epistle of the Holy Apostle Paul to the Thessalonians, Section 270 (1 Thess. 4:13–17):*

Brethren: I would not have you to be ignorant concerning them which are asleep, that ye sorrow not, even as others which have no hope. For if we believe that Jesus died and rose again, even so them also which sleep in Jesus will God bring with Him. For this we say unto you by the word of the Lord, that we which are alive and remain unto the coming of the Lord shall not prevent them which are asleep. For the Lord Himself shall descend from heaven with a shout, with the voice of the archangel, and with the trump of God: and the dead in Christ shall rise first: then we which are alive and remain shall be caught up together with them in the clouds, to meet the Lord in the air: and so shall we ever be with the Lord.

*The Gospel according to John, Section 16 (John 5:24–30):*

The Lord said unto the Jews which came unto Him: Truly, truly, I say unto you [f. 136A] in Himself; and hath given Him authority to execute judgment also, because He is the Son of man. Marvel not at this: for the hour is coming, in the which all that are in the graves shall hear the voice of the Son of God, and shall come forth; they that have done good, unto the resurrection of life; and they that have done evil, unto the resurrection of damnation. I can of Mine own self do nothing: as I hear, I judge: and My judgment is just; because I seek not Mine own will, but the will of the Father Which hath sent Me.

*Communion Verse: Rejoice in the Lord, O ye righteous. (Ps. 32:1) Another: Blessed are they whom Thou hast chosen and taken to Thyself, O Lord. (Ps. 64:5a)*

(see fol. 205)[^224]

*Resurrectional Troparia after the Amomos [Evlogitaria], Tone 5, with the Refrain:*

*Blessed art Thou, O Lord, teach me Thy statutes. (Ps. 118:12)*

*Troparion 1:*

The assembly of the Angels was amazed, beholding Thee numbered among the dead, yet destroying the power of death, O Savior, and raising up Adam with Thyself, and releasing all from hades.

*Troparion 2:*

Why do ye disciples mingle myrrh with compassionate tears? cried the radiant Angel in the tomb unto the myrrh-bearing women: behold the tomb and understand; for the Savior is risen from the tomb!

*Troparion 3:*

Very early the myrrh-bearing women ran unto Thy tomb lamenting; but an Angel stood before them and said: The time for lamentation is ended, weep not, but announce the Resurrection unto the Apostles!

*Troparion 4:*

The myrrh-bearing women, having come with myrrh unto Thy tomb, O Savior, were weeping; but the Angel spoke unto them, saying: Why do ye count the Living among the dead? For as God He is risen from the tomb!

*Glory...:*

Let us worship the Father, and His Son, and [f. 136A] the Holy Trinity in one essence, crying with the Seraphim: Holy, Holy, Holy art Thou, O Lord!

*Both now... Theotokion:*

Having given birth unto the Giver of life, O Virgin, thou didst deliver Adam from sin, and didst bestow joy unto Eve in place of sorrow; and He Who was incarnate from thee, God and man, directed toward this [life] those that had fallen away from life. Alleluia, [alleluia, alleluia; glory to Thee, O God] (thrice).

*Resurrectional Troparia, for every Sunday, after the Great Doxology:*

*On Tones 1, 3, 5, and 7, we say this:*

*Troparion:*

Today salvation is come unto the world: let us sing unto Him Who is risen from the tomb and is the Author of our life; for having destroyed death by death, He gave us the victory and great mercy.

*And on Tones 2, 4, 6, and 8, we say this:*

*Troparion:*

Having risen from the tomb and torn asunder the bonds of hades, Thou didst loose the condemnation of death, O Lord, delivering all from the snares of the enemy; and having shown Thyself unto Thine Apostles, Thou didst send them forth unto the preaching, and through them hast granted Thy peace unto the universe, O only Much-Merciful One.

(see fol. 206)[^225]

---

# EXAPOSTILARIA AND EVANGELICAL STICHERA

## RESURRECTIONAL EXAPOSTILARIA AND ELEVEN EVANGELICAL STICHERA (EOTHINA)

*Resurrectional Exapostilaria and Evangelical Stichera. The Work of Emperor Leo.*

*Exapostilarion 1:*

With the disciples let us go up into the mountain of Galilee with faith to behold Christ, Who says that He has received authority over things above and things below; let us learn how He teaches to baptize all nations in the name of the Father, and of the Son, and of the Holy Spirit, and to abide with the initiates of His mysteries, even as He promised, unto the end of the age.

*Theotokion:*

Thou didst rejoice with the disciples, O Virgin Mother of God, for thou didst behold Christ risen from the tomb on the third day, even as He said; unto whom also He appeared, teaching and revealing greater things, and commanding to baptize in the Father, and the Son, and the Spirit: that we may believe in His rising, and glorify thee, O Maiden.

*Glory... Evangelical Sticheron 1, Tone 1:*

When the disciples were going up into the mountain for the earthly ascension, the Lord stood before them, and they worshipped Him; and having been taught of the authority given Him everywhere, they were sent into all under heaven to preach the resurrection from the dead [f. 136A] and the return unto heaven; with whom Christ God, the truthful One, promised to abide everlastingly, He Who is the Savior of our souls.

*Exapostilarion 2:*

Beholding the stone rolled away, the myrrh-bearing women rejoiced, for they saw a young man sitting in the tomb, and he said unto them: Behold, Christ is risen! Say unto the disciples with Peter: Make haste unto the mountain of Galilee, and there He shall appear unto you, even as He told His friends beforehand.

*Theotokion:*

An Angel brought the "Rejoice" unto the Virgin before Thy conception, O Christ; and an Angel rolled away the stone of Thy tomb: the former instead of sorrow bringing tokens of ineffable joy; and the latter in place of death proclaiming and magnifying Thee as the Giver of life, and announcing the Resurrection unto the women and the initiates of the mysteries.

*Glory... Evangelical Sticheron 2, Tone 2:*

Unto the women that came with Mary bearing myrrh, and who were at a loss how their desire might be fulfilled, the stone appeared rolled away, and a divine young man, calming the turmoil of their souls, said:...

(see fol. 207)[^226]

...For Jesus the Lord is risen! Therefore proclaim unto His preachers, His disciples, to hasten unto Galilee, and ye shall see Him risen from the dead, as the Giver of life and the Lord.

*Exapostilarion 3:*

Let no one disbelieve that Christ is risen; for He appeared unto Mary, and then was seen of those going into the country; and He appeared again unto the Eleven disciples as they reclined at table; and having sent them to baptize, He was taken up into the heavens, whence also He had descended, confirming the preaching with multitudes of signs.

*Theotokion:*

O Sun that hast shone forth from the tomb today as a Bridegroom from his chamber, and having despoiled hades and abolished death: by the prayers of her that bore Thee send down light upon us, light enlightening our hearts, light directing all to walk in the paths of Thy commandments and in the way of peace.

*Glory... Evangelical Sticheron 3, Tone 3:*

When Mary Magdalene announced the Savior's resurrection from the dead and His appearance, the disciples, disbelieving, were reproached for the hardness of their hearts; but armed with signs and miracles, they were sent forth unto the preaching; and Thou, O Lord, wast taken up unto the primordial Light, the Father [f. 136A] while they preached the Word everywhere, confirming it with miracles; therefore, having been enlightened for their sake, we glorify Thy resurrection from the dead, O Lord and Lover of mankind!

*Exapostilarion 4:*

Radiant with virtues, let us behold two men standing in dazzling garments within the life-bearing tomb; and when the myrrh-bearing women bowed their faces to the earth, let us learn of the resurrection of Him Who rules the heavens; and let us run with Peter unto the tomb, unto life; and wondering at what was done, let us abide to behold Christ.

*Theotokion:*

Saying "Rejoice," Thou didst transform the sorrow of our forebears, O Lord, bringing the joy of Thy rising unto the world; therefore, O Giver of life, for the sake of her that bore Thee, do Thou, the Light that enlightens the heart, send forth the light of Thy compassions, that we may cry unto Thee, O Lover of mankind, God-Man: Glory to Thy rising!

*Glory... Evangelical Sticheron 4, Tone 4:*

At deep dawn the women came unto Thy tomb, O Christ, but the body they loved was not found; therefore, as they were at a loss...

(see fol. 208)[^227]

...men standing by in dazzling garments said: Why seek ye the Living among the dead? He is risen, as He said beforehand! Why remember ye not His words? Believing whom, they preached what was seen; but the glad tidings seemed as falsehood, so sluggish were the disciples still; but Peter ran and, beholding, glorified in himself Thy wonders!

*Exapostilarion 5:*

Christ, the Life and the Way, having risen from the dead, journeyed with Cleopas and Luke, by whom also He was recognized in Emmaus in the breaking of bread, whose souls and hearts were burning when He spoke unto them on the way and expounded from the Scriptures the things concerning His suffering; with whom, having risen, let us cry aloud: He has appeared unto Peter also!

*Theotokion:*

I hymn Thine unnumbered mercy, O my Creator; for Thou didst empty Thyself to bear and save afflicted human nature; and being God, Thou wast pleased to become like me from the holy Maiden of God, and to descend even unto hades, desiring me to be saved, by the prayers of her that bore Thee, O all-bountiful Master!

*Glory... Evangelical Sticheron 5, Tone 5:*

O the exceedingly wise judgments of Thine, O Christ! How unto Peter [f. 136A] by the linen clothes alone Thou gavest to understand Thy resurrection; and journeying with Luke and Cleopas, Thou didst converse with them, and conversing, didst not quickly reveal Thyself; therefore also Thou wast reproached as being the only stranger in Jerusalem who knew not to the end what had taken place therein; yet Thou Who disposest all things for the profit of the creature didst unfold the prophecies concerning Thyself, and in the blessing of the bread wast recognized by them, whose hearts even before that had burned within them for the knowledge of Thee; and they already clearly proclaimed Thy resurrection unto the assembled disciples: through them have mercy on us!

*Exapostilarion 6:*

Showing that Thou art man in essence, O Savior, having risen from the tomb and stood in their midst, Thou didst partake of food, and didst teach the baptism of repentance; and straightway Thou wast taken up unto the Heavenly Father, and didst promise to send the Comforter unto the disciples, O transcendent God-Man: Glory to Thy rising!

*Theotokion:*

The Creator of creation and God of all, having taken earthly flesh...

(see fol. 209)[^228]

...from thy most pure blood, O all-holy Virgin, renewed all of my decayed nature, leaving thee after childbearing even as thou wast before birth; therefore with faith we all praise thee, crying: Rejoice, O Lady, boast of the universe!

*Glory... Evangelical Sticheron 6, Tone 6:*

Thou art the true Peace, O Christ, unto God's men; giving Thy peace unto the disciples after the resurrection, Thou didst show them to be terrified, thinking that they saw a spirit; but Thou didst quiet the turmoil of their souls, showing Thy hands and Thy feet; yet while they still disbelieved, by partaking of food and reminding them of Thy teachings, Thou didst open their mind to understand the Scriptures; promising them also the Father's promise, and having blessed them, Thou didst ascend into heaven: therefore with them we worship Thee, O Lord, glory to Thee!

*Exapostilarion 7:*

When Mary said that they had taken away the Lord, Simon Peter and the other initiate of Christ's mysteries whom He loved ran unto the tomb; and they both ran, and found the linen clothes lying alone within, and the napkin that was about His head [f. 136A] was apart from them; therefore again they held their peace, until they saw Christ.

*Theotokion:*

Great and most glorious things hast Thou accomplished for my sake, O my most merciful Christ; for Thou wast born ineffably of the Virgin Maiden, and wast given over to the Cross, and didst endure death, and didst rise in glory, and didst deliver our nature from death: glory, O Christ, to Thy glory!

*Glory... Evangelical Sticheron 7, Tone 7:*

Lo, it was darkness and early, and why standest thou at the tomb, O Mary, having great darkness in thy mind? Wherein thou inquirest where Jesus has been laid; but behold the disciples who ran together: how from the linen clothes and the napkin they discovered the Resurrection, and remembered the Scriptures concerning Him; with whom and for whose sake having believed, we hymn Thee, Christ the Giver of life!

*Exapostilarion 8:*

Beholding two Angels within the tomb, Mary was amazed; and not knowing Christ, she questioned Him as a gardener: Sir, where hast Thou laid the body of my Jesus? But recognizing Him by His call to be the Savior Himself, she heard:...

(see fol. 210)[^229]

...Touch Me not, for I go unto the Father; tell My brethren!

*Theotokion:*

O Maiden, ineffably thou didst bear One of the Trinity, Who is in two natures and in two operations, and One in hypostasis; Him therefore pray unceasingly: that they who together with Him worship in faith may be delivered from every assault of the enemy; for we all now flee unto thee, O Mother of God!

*Glory... Evangelical Sticheron 8, Tone 8:*

Mary's tears were not shed warmly in vain; for behold, she was accounted worthy of angelic instruction and of the sight of Jesus Himself; yet she still thought of earthly things, as a weak woman; therefore also she was sent away not to touch Christ; but nevertheless she was sent as a herald unto Thy disciples, unto whom she proclaimed the good tidings, announcing Thine ascension unto the paternal inheritance: with her do Thou also account us worthy of Thine epiphany, O Master and Lord!

*Exapostilarion 9:*

When the doors were shut, O Master, as Thou didst enter, Thou didst fill the Apostles with the All-Holy Spirit, breathing peace upon them, unto whom Thou didst say to bind and loose sins [f. 136A] and after eight days Thou didst show Thy side and hands unto Thomas, with whom we cry: Thou art Lord and God!

*Theotokion:*

When thou sawest thy Son risen on the third day from the tomb, O all-pure Virgin, thou didst put away all sorrow which thou didst endure as a Mother when thou beheldest Him suffering; and filled with joy, glorifying Him with His disciples, thou didst hymn Him; therefore save those who now confess thee as Mother of God!

*Glory... Evangelical Sticheron 9, Tone 5:*

As at the end of days, on the evening of the Sabbath, Thou didst stand before Thy friends, O Christ, and by a miracle didst confirm a miracle: Thy resurrection from the dead by entering while the doors were shut; and Thou didst fill the disciples with joy, and didst impart unto them the Holy Spirit, and gavest them authority to forgive sins; and Thou didst not leave Thomas to be engulfed in the tempest of unbelief: therefore grant unto us also true understanding and forgiveness of transgressions, O compassionate Lord!

*Exapostilarion 10:*

The Sea of Tiberias of old had the sons of Zebedee...

(see fol. 211)[^230]

...with Nathanael and Peter, and two others, and Thomas, at fishing; who by Christ's command casting to the right side, drew a multitude of fishes; Whom Peter having recognized, swam toward Him, unto whom He appeared the third time, and showed bread, and fish upon the coals.

*Theotokion:*

Entreat the Lord Who rose on the third day from the tomb, O Virgin, for those who hymn thee and lovingly call thee blessed; for we all possess thee as a saving refuge and mediatress unto Him; for we are thy heritage and servants, O Mother of God, and we all look unto thy protection.

*Glory... Evangelical Sticheron 10, Tone 6:*

After the descent into hades and the resurrection from the dead, grieving as was fitting over Thy departure, O Christ, the disciples returned unto their labor: and again the boat, and the nets, and no catch at all; but Thou, O Savior, didst appear as Master of all, commanding to cast the net to the right side; and the word was straightway fulfilled in deed: and the multitude of fishes was great, and a strange supper was prepared upon the land; whereof Thy disciples then having partaken [f. 136A] do Thou now also account us worthy spiritually to enjoy, O Lord and Lover of mankind!

*Exapostilarion 11:*

After His divine rising, the Lord thrice said unto Peter: Lovest thou Me? He appointed him chief pastor over His sheep; who, seeing him whom Jesus loved following after, questioned the Master: But what shall this man do? If I will, He said, that he tarry till I come again, what is that to thee, friend Peter?

*Theotokion:*

O dread mystery! O most glorious wonder! By death, death has been utterly destroyed! Who therefore will not hymn, or who will not worship Thy resurrection, O Word, and her that in the flesh gave birth unto Thee, the Mother of God, by whose prayers deliver all from Gehenna!

*Glory... Evangelical Sticheron 11, Tone 8:*

Manifesting Thyself unto Thy disciples after the resurrection, O Savior, Thou gavest the pasturing of the sheep unto Simon as a reward of love, seeking pastoral care from him; therefore also Thou saidst: If thou lovest Me, Peter, feed My lambs, feed My sheep! But he straightway, showing his brotherly affection, questioned concerning the other disciple: through whose prayers, O Christ, preserve Thy flock from the wolves that destroy it!

(see fol. 212)[^231]

*Refrains of Gregory of Sinai:*

*Sung on all Sundays, after the Canon to the Trinity:*

*Refrain 1:*

It is truly meet in godlike manner to glorify Thee, O Holy Trinity, boundless and supersubstantial, and our all-good God, Whom all the earthborn hymn, and the heavenly hosts glorify, Who art faithfully worshipped by all: a Unity in essence, in three Hypostases.

*Refrain 2:*

Thee, the sole Principal of the Cherubim, and incomparable divine Origin of the Seraphim, the undivided Trinity in Unity, the existing divine Principle, do we magnify.

*Refrain 3:*

Unto the unoriginate Father and God, co-unoriginate with the Word and the Spirit, I bow down; the inseparable, single united Essence, the threefold Unity, let us honor with hymns!

*Refrain 4:*

Let us all sing in godlike manner with divine hymns unto the Father, and the Son, and the divine Spirit: the tri-hypostatic Dominion, one Kingdom and Lordship.

*Refrain 5:*

Make Thy radiant lightnings to shine upon me, O my God [f. 136A] tri-hypostatic Creator of all; and show me forth as an abode of Thine unapproachable glory, bright, light-bearing, and immutable.

*Refrain 6:*

Before Whom the Cherubim tremble and quake, and Whom the angelic armies glorify, Who was ineffably incarnate of the Virgin: Christ the Giver of life let us glorify with fear!

*Refrain 7:*

It is meet with all reverence to pray unto Thee with tears, the All-Merciful and All-Bountiful Savior of our souls.

*Refrain 8:*

Receive my tears, O King, accept the weeping and lamentation of the wretched one, and deliver me from the flame and outer darkness, and make me a partaker of the divine Kingdom.

*Glory...*

O Father Almighty, O Word of God, O All-Holy Spirit: one Power and Image, one Kingdom, Godhead, and Authority: have mercy and save those who worship Thee!

*Both now... Theotokion:*

Beholding thy Son risen in godlike manner from the dead, O all-pure Virgin, creation was filled with ineffable joy: glorifying Him, we magnify thee!

(see fol. 213)[^232]

*Sunday Resurrectional Prokeimena for each Tone:*

*Tone 1: Let Thy mercy, O Lord, be upon us, according as we have hoped in Thee. (Ps. 32:22)*

*Tone 2: The Lord is my strength and my song, and He is become my salvation. (Ps. 117:14)*

*Tone 3: Sing praises to our God, sing praises; sing praises unto our King, sing praises. (Ps. 46:7)*

*Tone 4: How magnified are Thy works, O Lord! In wisdom hast Thou made them all. (Ps. 103:24)*

*Tone 5: Thou, O Lord, shalt keep us, and shalt preserve us from this generation, and unto the age. (Ps. 11:8)*

*Tone 6: Save Thy people, O Lord, and bless Thine inheritance. (Ps. 27:9)*

*Tone 7: The Lord will give strength unto His people; the Lord will bless His people with peace. (Ps. 28:11)*

*Tone 8: Pray ye, and render vows unto the Lord our God. (Ps. 75:12)*

[f. 136A] [Folio 213v: Verso of final text folio. Preserves historical 18th-century cursive ownership and parish records in brown ink (partially rubbed: "Month of... Church warden / founder..."), later 19th/20th-century pencil notations, and a vernacular Ukrainian note in blue ink ("Anna Polishchuk..."). The lower third bears an antique paper repair patch displaying faint offset show-through of the facing leaf's hymnographic text.]

(see fol. 214)[^233]

[Folio 214r: Rear blank protective flyleaf. Heavy antique handmade laid paper without text, bearing light age-toning, natural patina, and faint circular waterstains.]

[f. 136A] [Folio 214v: Verso of rear flyleaf. Historical village registry and accounting inscriptions recorded in cursive Cyrillic in graphite pencil and blue ink: "Vasyl Mykhalchuk", "Vasyl Mykytiuk and Vasyl...", "Kmet...", "Hryts Kroliak 200", "Kateryna Savchuk", and family memorial names with cross: "Vasyl + Mykyta...". Lower left corner reinforced with antique paper patch.]

(see fol. 215)[^234]

[Folio 215r: Inside rear binding board (pastedown). Brown binding paper mounted onto the wooden board, preserving modern library catalogue and accession shelfmarks inked in black: "MKDU 13962 / SF 319". Concludes the physical leaves of the 17th-century manuscript Octoechos.]

[f. 136A] [Rear binding cover: Outside lower wooden board bound in dark brown calfskin with blind and gilt tooling. A continuous ornamental running foliate and wave roll border frames the perimeter; in the center is a blind- and gilt-stamped medallion of the Golgotha Cross on a stepped mount. Remnants of brass clasps and antique surface wear on the leather.][^235]

[f. 136A] [Spine of the codex: Dark brown calfskin over three raised bands dividing the spine into four compartments. The topmost compartment bears a pasted paper archival label in a printed double rectangular border with the number "57", accompanied by antique linen repair stitching at the upper joint. The third compartment contains a central blind-stamped fleur-de-lis palmette vignette within a blind-ruled frame. Worn tailcap and exposed cord supports at the joints, concluding the physical leaves of the manuscript.][^236]

# FOOTNOTES AND SCHOLARLY COMMENTARY



[^1]: The manuscript reads *Ди[по]полской* with the medial letters partially smudged or corrected in contemporary ink, designating a local parish church in the Carpathian/Galician eparchy.

[^2]: Dedicated to the Miracle of the Archangel Michael at Chonae (commemorated September 6).

[^3]: Recorded in Cyrillic numerals as *҂аѱз҃* (1707).

[^6]: Folio 4 begins on the facing leaf, numbered *д҃* in the upper right margin.

[^7]: The manuscript records only the opening incipit of the Heirmos (*Твоя побѣдителная десница. бголѣпно*).

[^8]: The scribe recorded the incipit of the Heirmos (*Единъ съвѣдый члвческаго сꙋщества немощь*).

[^9]: Folio 5 begins on the facing leaf, numbered *є҃* in the upper right margin. The sessional hymn is set to the model melody of Tone 1, *Гро́бъ тво́й, Спа́се*.

[^10]: Incipit of Ode 4 Heirmos (*Горꙋ тя блгодáтїю Бжїю присѣнъ*).

[^11]: Incipit of Ode 5 Heirmos (*Просвѣщенїемъ сїянїемъ пришествїя твоего*).

[^12]: Folio 6 begins on the facing leaf, numbered *ѕ҃* in the upper right margin.

[^13]: Incipit of Ode 6 Heirmos (*Ѡбыйде насъ послѣдняя беꙁдна*).

[^14]: Folio 7 begins on the facing leaf, numbered *з҃* in the upper right margin.

[^16]: Incipit of the Ode 8 Heirmos of the Trinity Canon (*Чудо превелїе, росодательную во образѣ пещь*).

[^17]: Folio 8 begins on the facing leaf, numbered *и҃* in the upper right margin.

[^20]: Folio 9 begins on the facing leaf, numbered *ѳ҃* in the upper right margin.

[^21]: Folio 10 begins on the facing leaf, numbered *і҃* in the upper right margin.

[^22]: Matins canon distribution rubric: "The Resurrection Canon on 4; the Cross-Resurrection Canon on 4; that of the Mother of God on 3; and in the Menaion on 4. If a celebrated saint, on 6." The manuscript codifies the standard Ruthenian synthesis of the three Octoechos Sunday canons with the daily Menaion.

[^24]: Incipit of the Katavasia of the Theotokos (*Отверзу уста моя*).

[^30]: The manuscript copyist wrote *прежде силъ* ("before the powers" or "unto the powers") in error for *прежде женамъ* ("first unto the women", cf. Mt 28:9).

[^31]: Folio 17 begins on the facing leaf, numbered *з҃і* in the upper right margin.

[^35]: The copyist's eye skipped while writing the fourth Beatitudes troparion (*Церковь Спасе телесную...*), mistakenly inserting phrases from the Tone 2 Anatolian sticheron (*...да, Ангелъ проповѣда...*) before resuming and completing the correct stanza on the next leaf.
